William Edward Hingston's Study


We have long known that William Edward Hingston (HN#26 or 540 in these notes) conducted a study of the Hingston family that he was planning to publish at the time of his death in 1906. It was being typed for him by Lavinia Hingston from his handwritten notes and part, which had been remained in Lavinia's possession after WEH's death, has been published on this site as the Vine Tree. No one knew what had happened to the rest of his papers so it had been assumed that they had been thrown away. Very recently, however, they have come into the possession of his great granddaughter, Florence McQuinn, who contacted me with a view to making his work more widely available.

What follows below is a transcript of those papers. I have added internal links, and at some stage in the future I will cross-link them to the trees available on this site. At the moment it should be regarded as a "work in progress". No links should be made to this page yet because its location is only temporary and it will change in the future.

Good starting points in what follows are:-

For Tree HA. 1500 Thomas (HA#4) was originally at the head of this tree. WEH's work allows us to go back to 1802 Henry (HA#37). Tree HA has now been updated to be consistent with WEH.

For Tree HC. Starts with 1575 Hugh (HC#6).

For Tree HD. WEH's line starts with 1. Andrew who is the same as our HD#1, but the subsequent line is disjointed because WEH was unaware of the Quaker records and sources like Allen and Dymond. There are multiple links here to Tree HD but no consistent thread.

For Tree HF. 1707 Joseph is the same as HF#1 but WEH goes back two further generations to 1701 Owen.

For Tree HH. Starts with 778 Philip (HH#40) and 780 John (HH#1). Most of this tree has already been sorted out and there are some discrepancies as noted in Tree HH.

For Tree HI. The existing tree starts with 1383 John (HI#1) but WEH goes back two further generations to 1361 John.

For Tree HL. Starts with 1101 Richard (HL#1) who may also be in Tree HH.

For Tree HM. Starts with 365. Baldwin (HM#8) but has a putative link to the Lyme Regis Hingstons through 957 William Henry.

For Tree HN. This has four subtrees that are not firmly linked.

  • HN#4 James is 202 James, a Major in Cromwell's Army and founder of the Aglish Branch (Tree HNA)
  • HN#10 Samuel is 701 Samuel, and is the founder of the Hingchingbrooke, Quebec Branch (Tree HNB)
  • HN#71 William is 501 William, founder of the Whitehall (Co Cork, Ireland) Branch (Tree HNC)
  • HN#49 Richard is 1933 Richard, founder of the Dunmanway (Co. Cork, Ireland) Branch (Tree HND).

WEH has possible links between these branches but I am uncertain how correct they are.

For Tree HO. There are considerable doubts about this line, with significant differences between what we have and what WEH had. His line starts with 1336 Robert and includes 1429 Edward Peron (HO#21)

For Tree HP. 201 John, Cromwell's Organist. (HP#2). His brother 301 Peter is the founder of Ipswich branch (HP#4)

For Tree HV. 430 Richard appears to be the founder of the Stoke Gabriel Branch (Tree HV)

There appear to be no links to Trees HK, HX and HY.

This transcript has not been checked. Text entries written by me are annotated "CJB". Things I can't decipher are marked with a double question mark (??). Things where I think the information is inconsistent, or which doesn't match with other things we know I have highlighted in red.

Copyright Notice

This material has been transcribed by Chris Burgoyne from papers supplied by Florence McQuinn who retains copyright. She intends to produce a book in due course. Members of the Hingston family and their descendants may include this information in their own family trees but it is not to be posted on the web without her permission. Under no circumstances is this information to be placed in any commercial database. Anyone taking information from this page is deemed to have agreed to these conditions.

This first list has clearly been added by WEH at the end of his study. It used a separate set of numbers that confusingly also started at No 1, so I have added the suffix "A" to each number to distinguish them from people in the main list. CJB

1A John Hingston

Alderman of London A. D. 1334-49, is the first of the family that I have any definite or positive record of at present, all previous to that date are traditionary. The said John Hingston was from Bigbury, Devonshire, the home of the Hingstons. Hingston, the then Manor house, is now a farm house (1902). John was apprenticed to Henry de la More, goldsmith, of London, whom he afterwards succeeded. He married Isabella La Bret(2000), and by her, who survived him, left two sons, Simon and William. At his death, in 1349, by will he left one-third of his estate to the church, one-third to his wife, and the other one-third to his two sons; and in the same document he makes his wife, Isabella, and his son Simon guardians of two of his kinsfolk, Guilbert Bordeyn and his sister Joanna, who were both under age. I also find that the said Guilbert and Joanna were called Hingston in the court calendar of Hastings (see copy below). From this I infer that they were the youngest children, and that "Bordeyn" was the wife's name. Among the ancient deeds in the Public Record Office I find the following:

"MIDDX. A. 2693. Grant by John de Hyngeston, goldsmith, and John de Tolenham, chandler, citizens of London, executors of the Will of Henry de la More, late citizen and goldsmith of London, to John Neirncut and Marrgary his wife, of the tenements that were the said Henry's in the parish of St Nicholas Olave in Old Fish Street, Friday the morrow of All Souls day, Edw.III (2 seals) (1 November, 1330)."

"MIDDX. A. 1879. Grant by Roland Schenc, son of Martin Sench deceased, to John de Hingeston, citizen and goldsmith of London, of the tenements with the garden he had by legacy from Martin his father in Aldersgate Street, in the parish of St. Botolph without, Aldersgate, London, situate as described, Tuesday after the Annunciation, 4 Edw. III, Seal (A. D. 1331)."

"MIDDX. A. 2390. Grant by John Neirncut to John de Hingestone, citizen and goldsmith, of London, of one more yearly quit rest he has by gift of Cristina, late the wife of William Le Bret, late citizen and goldsmith of London, &c. &c. Sunday after St. Peter (ad vincula) 4 Edw.III (A. D. 1331)."

"MIDDX. A. 2654. Letters of attorney of John Nierncut empowering Thomas Neirncut his brother, to put John de Hyngeston in possession of above. Sunday after St. Peter in Cathedra 4 Edw.III, (A. D. 1331)."

MSS. sent me by the Rev. Richard Edward Hull Hingston of Aglish, now of London (1902):

"John de Hyngeston (goldsmith), Sheriff 1334. He is first rnentioned as an alderman in 1334, Sheriff 1334 with Walter Burke, who was Mayor in 1350. The same notice also appears in the first recognized list of the common council as one of the six members for Cripplegate, 1347. This was probably a son of the alderman of whose Will an extract is here given: 'Hyngeston (John de), goldsmith. The residue of debits due to him to be divided into three parts,whereof one part is to be devoted to the good of his soul; the second part to go to Isabella his wife, and the other third to his children. To Simon, his son, entail, a tenement in Wodestreet, which he purchased of the executors of Henry otte More, provided that his own debts shall have been satisfied by the payment of what is due to him,; remainder to William, his son, in tail. The said Isabella and Simon de Worstede (who was then alderman of Cripplegate), appointed guardians of Gilbert Bordeyn, kinsman, and Joanna, sister of the same Gilbert, until they shall come of age. Dated at Wodestreet Palace Sunday, April 5, 1349.' Copied by Rev. R. Hingston from the Aldermen of Applegate Ward. N.B. In the Court Calendar of Hastings, the above Gilbert and Joanna appear as Hyngestone."

"MIDX. A. 1959. Release by Roland Schench, son of Martin Schenck, deceased, to John de Hyngeston, citizen and goldsmith of London, of the tenements, with the garden in Aldersgatestrete, in the parish of St. Botolph without, Aldersgate, which he had by bequest of his father and which the said John acquired of him. Saturday, the eve of Easter, 8 Edward III (Seal) (A. D. 1335)."

"MIDDX. B. 2012. John de Hingston, Sheriff of London, witness to grant, Monday after Exaltation of the Holy Cross, 9 Edw. III (A. D. 1336).

"MIDDX. A. 2066. John de Hingston, alderman of London, witness to a release deed, Sunday after All Saints day, 9 Edw. III, (A. D. 1336).

2A Robert Hingston

Of Hingston Co., Devon, resident of London, brother to John 1A. married in 1311, to Alice, daughter and heiress of Le Moyne Baldwin, 2198, (see Monk's Pedigree), and by her had three children, Richard 3A, Gilbert 18A, and Joanna 19A. He must have died early, for his two youngest were not of age when their uncle died in 1349.

3A Richard Hingston

Son of Robert 2A and Alice 2198, born 1312, and married in 1355, to Jane Beckebarce 2199, daughter of William Beckebarce, who left her lands in 28 Edw.III (A. D. 1354).

4A Hugh Hingston.

In the Visitations of Devon the name is spelled Hindeston. He died without issue. Was eldest son of Richard 3A and Jane 2199. Was born 1356.

5A William Hingston

Second son (presumably of Richard 3A - CJB) and heir to Hugh 4A. Was Sergeant at Law. Born 1370, and was alive 2 Feb. 1466 - an extract from Ancient deeds in the Public Record Office; "(Devon) C. 127. Indenture of settlement by which Henry Drew ratifies and confirms the estate and possessions of John Hyll &c. &c. of the manors Schyl1yston, Sprydllyston &c. &c. The deed recites that William Hundeston, Sergeant at Law, then deceased, and Henry Drew, stood seized of the premises &c., and that the said William Hyndeyston and Henry Drew conveyed the estate &c. a February, 5 Edw , IV (1466).

6A Robert Hingston

Son of William 5A (original says 4A), born A. D. 1400, at Wonwell , Co. Devon, married Margaret 2201, who survived him and married William Ash 2203, who died 17 June, 1508. She was spoken of as the widow of Robert Hingston in 1511, and as her first son was born in 1425 she must then have been over 100 years old. Robert 6A dieing after 1435 left children, Robert(7A), Agnes(8A), William(9A) and Elizabeth(10A)

7A Robert Hingston

(In Harl. M.S.1080 he is called Philip), son of Robert 6A and Margaret 2201, born 1425. Died leaving two sons, Phillip(11A) and William(12A).

8A Agnes Hingston

(called Philipa in Harl. M.S.), eldest daughter of Robert 6A and Margaret 2201, married her cousin Robert Ashford 2204 of Ashford in 1461. Her second son was steward to Robert Carey of Cockington in 1506. The elder son, Robert Ashford of Wonwell married Agnes, daughter of T. Fortescue, and died 11 March, 1501.

9A William Hingston

Second son of Robert 6A and Margaret 2201, had two daughters, Elizabeth 13A and Margaret 14A.

10A Elizabeth Hingston

Youngest daughter of Robert 6A and Margaret 2201, born at Wonwell about 1440, married in 1460 to Sir Phillip Courtenay 2184, who had Molland given him for his portion. They had issue three sons and two daughters. Their eldest son, John 3170, succeeded his father in his estate, married Joan, daughter of Robert Prett of Pillond in Pelton parish, and died 27 March 1509; was buried in Molland church. From him through several generations descended the Courtenay family and founders of the Prideaux family. Sir. Phillip's eldest daughter, Elizabeth 3172, was married to her cousin, Sir Edward Courtenay, in 1842, who was restored to the title of Earl of Devon 1485. He, Sir Edward (sometimes called Sir William), was knighted on the field by the Earl of Richmond 7 August 1485; created Earl of Devon 26 October 1485; bearer of the second sword at the coronation of Henry VII, 30 October 1485; Justice of the Peace County of Somerset 20 November 1485.; a Commisioner of the Royal Mines in England and Wales 27 February 1486; Constable of Restomell Castle and Keeper of the Park 1 March 1486; Chief Commissioner to muster archers in County Connwal1 23 December 1488; Knight of the Garter July 1489; Captain in the King's army (France) September to November 1492; Commissioner in Oyer and Terminer in London and Middlesex 16 February 1494; First Commissioner to inquire into Rebellion in Cornwall and Devonshire, Somerset, Dorset, Wiltshire, Hannts., Surrey and Gloucester 28 June 1497; made Captain of the Royal army (west) September 1497; repulsed Perkin Welbeck's attack on Exeter 1497; died 1 November 1509, and is buried in Tiverton. Their son William married the Princess Catherine, sister of Elizabeth, wife of King Henry VII; was made Knight of the Bath 1487, and died 9 January 1511. His wife, Princess Catherine, died 15 November 1527. Sir Phillip of Molland, 2184, was High Sheriff of Devonshire 10 Edward IV (1471), and his family continued in a flourishing condition to the year 1732, when John Courtenay of Molland died without issue.

11A Phillip Hingston

Of Wonwell, eldest son of Robert 7A.

12A Richard Hingston

Of Wonwell, second son of Robert 7A.

13A Elizabeth Hingston

Eldest daughter of William 9A. She was born at Wonwell 1465, and married William Fortescue 2187, Gentlemen of Wood, who died 22 July 1520 (Inquisition Post Mortem 13 HenryVIII) A. D. 1522.

14A Margaret Hingston

Second daughter of William 9A was born about 1467, and married John Fortescue of Fallepet in 1509. The two Fortescues, William and John, were cousins (see Fortescue Pedigree). Their only daughter, Elizabeth 3304, born 1510, married her cousin, Lewis Fortescue, one of the Barons of the Exchequer. Their son John, born 1525, died 1595, married Honoria, daughter of Edmund Specott, who was born 1528 and died 1616, leaving issue Edmund, born 1552, Lewis Fortescue, who married Elizabeth 3304, daughter of Margaret 14A, was bred to the bar, was successively one of the readers of the Middle Temple, Sergeant at Law, and a Baron of the Exchequer. He was raised to the bench about the close of the reign of Henry VIII.

15A Elizabeth Hingston

Daughter of Philip 11A, married John Coppen, 2205, of Portledge in 1456 (see Visitation of Devon 1564).

16A Simon Hingston de Worstede

Eldest son of John 1A and Isabella 2000. Have no further trace other than that he was alderman for Cripplegate in 1349, and appointed guardian with his mother over Guilbert 18A and Joana 19A.

17A William Hingston

Second son (of John 1A and Isabella 2000? CJB). Have no further trace.

18A Gilbert Burdeyn Hingston

19A Joana Hingston

His sister, spoken of in the Will of John Hingston 1A

20A Richard Hingston

Born about 1500. B.A. 3 February 1522-3.

21A William Hingston

Born 1590, married 11 December 1616, to Martha 2207, daughter of Arthur Arscott and Mary Langford of Telcott. After his death, his wife married Sir William Button, Knight, of Felham.

22A John Hingston.

23A Elizabeth Hingston.

24A. Elizabeth Hingston

Daughter of John 22A, who was remembered in the will of John Mortymore of Stokingham along with her two children, Robert 25A and Helene 26A; presumably the widow and children of ... Mortymore of Stokingham. Will dated 16 May, 1633.

25A Robert Mortymore Hingston?

26A Helene Mortymore? Hingston?

Son and daughter of Elizabeth Hingston 24A, daughter of John Hingston, mentioned in the Will of Joan Mortymore of Stokingham, dated 16 May 1633.

27A John Hingston

Of Totness, whom I believe to be identical with John 22A, the father of Elizabeth 24A mentioned above. He was born about 1520, and had a son, John 28A.

28A John Hingston

Of Totness, son of John 27A-22A, was born 1544, and martriculated at Balliol College, Oxford, 19 July 1552 (see John Hingston of Dartmouth 1361).

This is the main sequence of numbers that form the bulk of the study.

1 Andrew Hingston

Of Wonwell son of Robert 20A moved to Holbeton on the west side of the Erme. His descendants became Lords of the Manor of Holbeton. He was the furthest back that I could go on New Years Day 1890 but the day 1 Jan 1902 I am able with the researches I have made and the assistance of Mr Cullerton of the Heraldry Office of London to go back to John Hingston who was born in the 13th Century. Andrew Hingston lived prior to and about AD 1600 and was succeeded by Walter 2 for his other two sons John 101 and William 102.

2 Walter Hingston

Of Holbeton son of Andrew 1 of Wonwell born 1566 married Sabilla ... 2538 and died 27 Oct 1627 leaving a son Andrew 3, Peter 301, James 103 and John 105. (The names of Peter, James and John have been added later but in the same handwriting - CJB) (Andrew 3) who succeeded him. His wife died 23 January 1635.

3 Andrew Hingston

Of Scotscombe, Dodbrooke House and Holberton son of Walter 2 and Sabilla 2538 was born in 1596 married in 1623 to Grace Hingston 200 his cousin of Ringmore and by her had six children Walter 4, James 5, Josias 6, Able 7, William 8 and Jane 9. He died 21 April 1643.

4 Walter Hingston

Son of Andrew 3 and Grace 200 born 1626 married in 1652 to Sarah Rowe 2001 and had issue one son Andrew 130, dieing (??) 8 July 1684. Among the Domestic State papers (Charles II) I find this. 1668 Jan 8 Ostend:- Declaration by Walter Hingston, merchant of Lyme, that the "Mary" of Guernsey passing from St Malo to Topsham, was seized by the "Fortune" of Ostend and the Master Peter Cannivette (??), with the declarant, sent to Ostend notwithstanding their passport, that by the needless delays of the Captain of the "Fortune" the vessel taken, struck and was lost, whereby he and many others lost their goods on board, and their papers and they protest against this unjust interception. (S.P. Dom. Car II 232, No 66)

5 James Hingston

Son of Andrew 3 and Grace 200 born 1626 married Alice 2002 about 152 and left issue James 10 and Andrew 11 from whome are descended the Hingstons of Holbeton, Dodbrook and Plymouth. He died 1659.

6 Josias Hingston

Third son of Andrew 3 and Grace 200 born 3 July 1621 and died 23 Oct 1707.

7 Able Hingston

Fourth son of Andrew 3 and Grace 200 born at Holbeton 30 July 1637 married 17 Nov 1662 at Thurlestone to Honor Cornish 2540 who died 21 June 1673. He died 1676 leaving three sons and two daughters Susanna 141, Able 142, Joseph 143, Grace 144 & Philip 145.

8 William Hingston

Fifth son of Andrew 3 and Grace 200 born 20 Dec 1640 at Holbeton and married to Margaret 2542 leaving issue Josias 138 = 1200. He died 28 July 1669.

9 Jane Hingston

Third child and only daughter of Andrew 3 and Grace 200 born 25 April 1629.

10 James Hingston

Eldest son of James 5 and Alice 2002 born 26 April 1654 married Ann 2534. She died 3 Sept 1726 and he died 1733 leaving issue James 12, Ann 154, William 155, Andrew 157, Henry 164 & Elizabeth 165, three girls and a boy having died young. (Mary 156, Joseph 162, Agnes 163 and Mary 166 have been crossed out but seem to be the children who died young - CJB)

11 Andrew Hingston

Second son of James 5 and Alice 2002 born 3 Feb 1660.

12 James Hingston

Son of James 10 and Ann 2534 born 30 May 1710 and married Annie Rowe 2003(??) who died without issue, and by his second marriage to Elizabeth Brooking 2004 who died in 1746 he had a son John 14 and a daughter Sarah 13.

13 Sarah Hingston

Only daughter of James 12 and Elizabeth 2004. Married William Hele 2005.

14 John Hingston

Banker of Kingsbridge son of James 12 and Elizabeth 2004 born in 1737 and married in 1760 to Rachel daughter of George Fox of Par, Co Cornwall, she dieing the following year 1761. He married secondly in 1763 Rachel 2007 daughter of Joseph Collier Esq of Plymouth and by her who died 1824 had with a daughter Dorothy 16 a son Joseph 15.

15 Joseph Hingston

Of Dodbrook son of John 14 and Rachel 2007 born in 1764. Married 22 Nov 1785 Sarah 2008 daughter of Joseph Ball of Bridgewater and by her who died in 1790 had issue Joseph 17 and Sarah Ball 18. He married secondly in 1796 Catherine Phillips 2009 daughter of Joseph Tregelles of Falmouth and by her had issue Frederick Collier 19, Charles 20, Alfred 21, Catherine Tregelles 22, Rachel Collier 23, Susanna Ann 24, Sophia Price 25 and Louisa Ellen 26. He died 30 April 1835.

16 Dorothy Hingston

Daughter of John 14 and Rachel 2007 married in 1790 to Robert Ware Fox 2010 who was half brother to her father's first wife Rachel Fox of Par 2006. She left issue Robert Ware Fox 2500 and Dorothy Fox 2501.

17 Joseph Hingston

Of Dodbrook House and Holbeton. Eldest son of Joseph 15 and Sarah 2008, born 5 May 1788. Married 8 Sep 1825 to Elizabeth Talwin 2011 daughter of James Kenway Esq of Bridgeport and had three daughters. Eliza Ann 28 who died young, Caroline Elizabeth 29 who died 12 Jan 1834 and Josephine 27. He was formerly Lord of the Manor of Holbeton Co Devon, but sold the estate to John Bulteel of Pamfleet. He died 1852.

18 Sarah Ball Hingston

Eldest daughter of Joseph 15 and Sarah 2008, born at Dodbrooke House, Kingsbridge 1786. Married in 1805 to Walter Prideaux (2012) of Kingsbridge who died 24 Jun 1832. She survived him 34 years and died at Kingsbridge 20 Dec 1866.

19 Frederick Collier Hingston

Son of Joseph 15 and Catherine 2009, born 1803 died 1810.

20 Charles Hingston

M.D. of Plymouth. Son of Joseph 15 and Catherine 2009 born 1805 and married in 1830 to Mary Braithwaite 2013 of Kendal and by her had two daughters Marianna 30 and Georgiana Braithwaite 31. By his second marriage to Lady Louisa Jane Parker 2014 in 1837 he had seven children, two boys and five girls. Charles Albert 32, Ernest Allison 33, Louisa 34, Charlotte Parker 35, Fannie Catherine 36, Clara Gertrude 37 and Sophia Elizabeth 38. Mr Charles Hingston (died) 5 Sep 1872.

21 Alfred Hingston

Of Plymouth and Marsh House, son of Joseph 15 and Catherine 2009 born 1807. Married in 1831 to Mary Nottage 2015 of Lancaster and by her had 11 children, five boys and 6 girls. Alfred Nottage 39, Joseph Tregillis 40, Frederick Collier 41, George 42, Jane Catherine 43, Mary Elizabeth 44, Ester Margaret 45, Emma Rachel 46, Rosetta 47, Augustus 48 and Lucy 71.

22 Catherine Tregillis Hingston

Daughter of Joseph 15 and Catherine 2009 born born at Marsh House, Plymouth 1797. Married in 1809 to Mr Brown 2016 of Longbourne, moved to Torquay. She died in 1888.

23 Rachel Collier Hingston

Daughter of Joseph 15 and Catherine 2009 born at Marsh House 1799. Married in 1819 to George Fox 2017, Banker of Wadebridge and had two children Edwin Fox and Frederick Hingston Fox. She died in 1885.

24 Susanna Anna Hingston

Daughter of Joseph 15 and Catherine 2009 born 1801 died 1843, never married.

25 Sophia Price Hingston

Daughter of Joseph 15 and Catherine 2009 born 1813. Married in 1836 to Alfred Gilkes of London, died in 1852.

26 Louisa Ellen Hingston

Son of Joseph 15 and Catherine 2009 born 1814. Married in 1835 to Guilbert Gilkes of London and died 1881.

27 Josephine Hingston

Of Dodbrook, daughter of Joseph Hingston 17 of Dodbrook House and Elizabeth 2011. Married in 1851 to Robert Dymond of Exeter and has a son Arthur Hingston Dymond.

28 Eliza Ann Hingston

Daughter of Joseph 17 and Elizabeth 2011. Died young.

29 Caroline Elizabeth Hingston

Of Dodbrook daughter of Joseph 17 and Elizabeth 2011 died 12 Jan 1834.

30 Marianne Hingston

Of Plymouth daughter of Charles Hingston 20 and Mary 2013 his wife born 1831. Died at Stafford 26 Feb 1870 buried in Plymouth Cemetery.

31 Georgiana Braithwaite Hingston

Daughter of Charles 20 and Mary 2013 born 1833. Died 12 Oct 1854 and buried in Plymouth Cemetery Pennycumquick.

32 Charles Albert Hingston

M.A. of Plymouth, son of Charles 20 and Louisa 2014 born 1843, and married in 1880 to Agnes Fanny Stewart 2297 of Plymouth who died in 1885 having one daughter Altha Stewart Hingston (49) born in 1881 and died in 1882.

33 Ernest Allison Hingston

Of Bristol, son of Charles 20 and Louisa 2014 born 1844. Married in 1869 to Mary Ellen Davis 2298 who died in 1895.

34 Louisa Hingston

Daughter of Charles 20 and Louisa 2014 born 1838 died 1 Feb 1858.

35 Charlotte Parker Hingston

Daughter of Charles 20 and Louisa 2014 born 1841. Married 1862 to William Fox 2266 of Nottingham; she died 13 Jun 1872, buried at Plymouth.

36 Fannie Catherine Hingston

Of Harrow on the Hill, daughter of Charles 20 and Louisa 2014 born 1846. Married in 1867 to A.J.Hill.

37 Clara Gertrude Hingston

Of Dublin, daughter of Charles 20 and Louisa 2014 born 1848. Married her cousin George Hingston 42, Col of HM Customs in 1873.

38 Sophia Elizabeth Hingston

Of Hamstead daughter of Charles 20 and Louisa 2014 born 1852. Married the Rev J Besley of Hamstead.

39 Alfred Nottage Hingston

M.A. eldest son of Alfred 21 of Marsh House and Mary 2015 his wife born 1832. Matric. 16 Oct 1850 B.A. and M.A. St Mary's Hall 1858 and Vicar of Churchstow Devon 1866-82. He married in 1872 Mary Jane 2021 daughter of the late Thomas Harris Esq of Kingsbridge. In St Edmunds Church of Kingsbridge there is a brass plate under a stained glass window with the inscription. "This window is presented by the youngest daughter of Thomas and Harriet Harris in affectionate remembrance of much loved parents, their brothers and sisters. A.N.Hingston Vicar 1873" Mr Hingston died 1890. In St Andrews Church Plymouth the writer saw the following words printed in the vestibule. "The present tenor bell was cast in London and hung in the tower 17 July 1840. It has the following inscription. (This bell was founded by Thomas Mears of London AD 1840). John Hatchard MA Vicar, Frederick Bone Churchwarden, Alfred Hingston Churchwarden. Weight 35 hundred 14 lbs. Breadth 4.11 1/2 height 3.3 1/2". I also found his name as J.P. and Treasurer upon the base of the "Armada Monument" on the Hoe at Plymouth.

40 Joseph Tregillis Hingston

Son of Alfred 21 and Mary 2015 born 1835. Married in 1868 to Emily Smith 2804 and has issue. Alfred Alwyn 79, Herbert Tregillis 80, Ethel Mona 81, Gerald Haynes 82, Winifred Emily Mary 83, Douglas Tregelles 84, Egerton Melville 85, Muriel Catherine 86 & Cicil Victor 86.

41 Frederick Collier Hingston

Third son of Alfred 21 and Mary 2015 born 1837 and died 1895.

42 George Hingston

Collector of HM Customs Dublin. 4th son of Alfred 21 and Mary 2015 born 1839 and married in 1873 to his cousin Clara Gertrude Hingston 37.

43 Jane Catherine Hingston

Of Plymouth eldest daughter of Alfred 21 and Mary 2015 born 1833.

44 Mary Elizabeth Hingston

Of Plymouth second daughter of Alfred 21 and Mary 2015 born 1834 died 1851.

45 Esther Margaret Hingston

Third daughter of Alfred 21 and Mary 2015 born at Plymouth 1838.

46 Emma Rachel Hingston

Fourth daughter of Alfred 21 and Mary 2015 born at Plymouth 1840.

47 Rosetta Hingston

Youngest daughter of Alfred 21 and Mary 2015 was married in 1866 and died three years afterwards in 1869.

48 Augustus Hingston

Of Totnes. Youngest son of Alfred 21 and Mary 2015 born at Plymouth 1849 married 1875. Is an attorney of prominence. Once Mayor of Totnes and Steward of several estates himself(??) owning considerable landed property.

49 Altha Stewart Hingston

Of Plymouth. Daughter of Charles 32 and Agnes 2297 his wife. Born 1881. Died 1882.

50 John Hingston

Of Stokenham. Father of John 55, Margaret 56, Thomas 57, Harold 58 and Joan 59. I know nothing further of him than that he was father of the above and alive in 1676.

51 Elizabeth Hingston

Of Stokenham, presumably sister of the above 50 was married 19 Feb 1665 to William Bullrum 2322 of Kingsbridge.

52 Grace Hingston

Daughter of William 63 married 22 Nov 1680 to Nicholas Pounds of Kingsbridge.

53 Thomas Hingston

Of Stokenham married Elizabeth ... 2666 who died in 1670 and left two sons and one daughter Susanna 61, (A daughter) 62 and John 64.

54 Stephen Hingston

Son of William 63 married 6 Oct 1685 to Richard Combs 2324 and had issue Sibella 65 and William 66. (There is a pencil note in a different hand saying "both men" but I have seen other cases in Devon where Richard is used for a female name - CJB)

55 John Hingston

Son of John 50 born Stokenham 15 Oct 1662.

56 Margaret Hingston

Daughter of John 50 born 1 May 1666.

Son of John 50 born 1 May 1666.

Son of John 50 born 30 Mar 1673.

Daughter of John 50. Married 2 Jun 1702 J Udy(??) 2325.

Son of William 63 married 15 Dec 1700 to Elizabeth Foot 2326.

61 Susanna Hingston

Daughter of Thomas 53 born 9 Jun 1664.

62 (A daughter) Hingston

A daughter of Thomas 53 who died 7 May 1663.

63 William Hingston

Of Stokenham who died 15 July 1688 father of William 60, Grace 52 and Stephen 54.

64 John Hingston

Son of Thomas 53 died 1 Jun 1669

65 Sebilla Hingston

Daughter of Stephen 54 and Richard 2324 born 1 Jan 1686

A son of Stephen 54 and Richard 2324 born 9 Apr 1695. Married 18 May 1727 to Catherine Effer.

67 Thomas Hingston

Son of Thomas 57 born 18 Nov 1707. Married Mary 2206

Daughter of William 60 and Elizabeth 2326 born 17 Jul 1707. Married 22 Dec 1726 to George Gillard.

Daughter of William 60 and Elizabeth 2326 born 25 Apr 1704. Married 10 Mar 1719 to Joseph Combes (This seems improbable - she would only have been 15. - CJB)

70 Agnes Hingston

Daughter of William 60 and Elizabeth 2326 born 26 Jan 1720.

71 Lucy Hingston

201 John Hingston

(HP#2) Of Ringmore Co Devon. (See comment at the bottom of this entry - CJB) Composer and Organist the son of Walter 2 (text says Walter 3) and Sabella 2538 born at Wonwell Co Devon about 1600 was a musician in the service successively of Charles I, of Cromwell and Charles II. He was a pupil of Orlando Gibbons and it is said by Hawkins that he was Dr Blow's earliest master. After the Protector brought the Magdalen College (Oxford) Organ to Hampton Court in 1654. He would listen with delight to Dearing's songs performed by Hingston and two boys. Cromwell's daughters took lessons in music from Hingston and Cromwell himself would frequently enjoy music at Hingston's house.

Sir Roger L'Estrange in his "Truth and Loyalty Vindicated", 1662 writes "Being in St James' Park I heard an organ touched in a late low room from Mr Hingston's. I went in and found a private company of five or six persons; they desired me to take up a viol and bear a part. I did so. By and by, without the least color of a design or expectation in comes Cromwell. He found us playing, and as I remember so he left us."

From 1661 to 1666 Hingston was among the gentlemen of the Chapel Royal; in July 1663 his office is specified as "Keeper of ye organs" and among the Domestic State Papers 1660-1670 I find the following "1670 Jun 14 Warrant for £130 to John Hingston for repair of the Organ in Whitehall Chapel, out of the privy seal dormant of 27 July last for £10,000 minute (S.P.Dom.Entry Book 34.fol.33).

His salary was £100 per year. Hingston gave his portrait to the Oxford Music School where it was hanging a short time ago. He wrote "Fancies for the Viol". A few of his compositions are preserved in the British Museum addit MS 31436 (1) a set of 12 fantasies named from the months in four parts; (2) a set of four fantasias, ayres, and galliards named from the seasons, in four parts & (3) Fantasias and almands for three bass viols (4) a manuscript set of fancies in six parts is in the music school Oxford.

It is said by some historians that he went over to the Protector for the salary. Now it is possible that the salary was an inducement during those hard times to enter the employ of Cromwell. But that he went over to Cromwell in a political sense I must deny for we find him still in office under the second Charles and his son James who was a Major under Cromwell also served under Charles II and William III.

Sam Pepys who in his "Diary" under date of the 10 day of December 1667 which was after the Restoration speaks of him thus. "Met Mr Hingston the Organist walking and I walked with him and asked him many questions. I do find that he can no more give an intelligible answer to a man that is not a great master in his art that another man. And this confirms me that it is only the want of an ingenious man that is master in music to bring music to a certainty and ease in Composition"

ibid 9 Dec 1666 "Talking of the King's family with Mr Hingston the Organist. He says many of the music are ready to starve they being five years behind hand for their wages; nay, Evans the famous man upon the harp having not his equal in the world did the other day die for near want, and was fain to be buried at the Alms of the parish and carried to his grave in the dark at night without one link but that Mr Hingston met it by chance and did give 12d to buy two or three links."

Mr Hingston had with others one son James Major in the army. He died 17 Dec 1683 and was buried in St Margaret's Church Westminster. His nephew Peter Hingston 313 (b. 1721) was teacher and organist at Ipswich.

(The description here of John Hingston's music accords with modern research about his life, but the genealogical connections both earlier and later do not. John is known to have been born in York, probably in about 1608, almost certainly the son of Rev Thomas Hingston, Vicar of St Lawrence, York. There is no evidence that he ever married or had children. In his will he made provision for a number of nephews and nieces and it is almost inconceivable that he would not have made provision for children, if he had any. The widely held view amongst the Irish branch that James Hingston was John's son could be explained by the fact that James was another of John's nephews, but again there is no evidence (yet) for that. We need to look in Yorkshire though, rather than Devon, for such a link. See Tree HP#2 for more details. CJB)

202 James Hingston

(HN#4)James Hingston the son of John 201 born in Ringmore (See comment above about this link - CJB) was Major in the Army under Cromwell, Charles II and William III and saw service in Ireland and Flanders and through his services there was awarded the Coat of Arms now used by the Irish branch of the family, that is, three leopards heads surrounding a chevron ermine on a blue field. Motto "Deum posui adjutorem" with the Stewart Lion for a crest. His son (James - 203 CJB) who was in the army with him bought the Aglish Estate in Ireland.

203 James Hingston

(HN#6) Son of Major James Hingston 202 and Grandson of John Hingston 201 the Organist who being appointed to the charge of a branch of the Commissariat Department in Ireland went over to that country where he settled after his retirement from the service he purchased at the great sale in April 28 1703 the estate of Aglish (or Aglis) (Irish for Church) in the Barony of Muskerry Co Cork, 677 acres for £829.£/- = $5980.00. This estate was forfeited by Tiege MCormic MCarthy in the rebellion of 1641 valued at £350 per annum. It lays on the south side of the river Lee about 16 miles from Cork. Mr Hingston married Helen (2020) daughter of Mr Alderman Morley in the City of Cork and by her had issue William 204 and Justinian 205.

204 William Hingston

206 James Hingston Rev.

(HN#8) The eldest son of William 204 and Elizabeth 2023 born 10 Nov 1713 was Rector of Donoghmore and Vicar of Clonmen Co Cork, also J.P. He married in 1741 Katherine (2024) daughter of the Rev Benezer Murdock rector of Kilshannig Co Cork by Elizabeth his wife, daughter of Herbert Love Esq of Cork and granddaughter of Judith the eldest daughter of Sir Philip Percival (of the ancient House of Yvery)(Norman) great grandfather of John first Earl of Egmont. The said Judith was married at Skull Co Cork in 1653 to Col. Randolph Clayton of Mallow. Through this marriage the names of Love; Clayton; and Percival were first brought into the Hingston family see numbers 221, 234, 248, 249, 253, 262, 263, 272 & 277. Mr James Hingston had by this marriage had five sons and five daughters viz. William 208; Benezer 209; James 210; John 211; Samuel 701; Catherine 212; Helen 213; Elizabeth 214; Mary 215 and Isabella 216. Mr Hingston compiled a statistical account of the Diocese of Cloyne in the year 1774. The voluminous manuscripts left by him show him to have been a man of patient application. He also wrote an abridgement of the statistics in three large quarto volumes for his own use as Justice of the Peace of the Co Cork. The most curious feature of which is the penmanship it is throughout written in Roman characters of great neatness to resemble ordinary typography. His usual writing was an imitation of italic print.

And a list of the students who matriculated in Trinity College Dublin with all particulars relating to them as entered in the College books. He also left with many others a prose translation of the "Odyssey of Homer". His writings have been distributed among his family and are highly prized among them. From the entry of his own matriculation it appears that Mr Hingston entered College as a pensioner on the 10 Nov 1729 aged 16, that he was born at Aglish in the Co. Cork was the son of William Hingston gentleman and received his school education at Cork under the Rev Edmond Malory who appears to have been the principal school master there at that period. His birthplace "Aglish" was an estate acquired by his grandfather in 1703. Smith writing in 1749 says "Aglish" is on the south side of the River Lee where there is a good house and plantation of Mr Hingston Vol 1 page 27 and at page 30 he mentions Mr Hingston as residing at Kilpadder.

The Cork Eagle, writing up the various parishes of Co Cork in 1810 thus writes "Book II Chap II - Part XX" "The Parish of Aglish lies on both sides of the river Lee; the south side in the diocese of Cork and the north side in that of Cloyne. Aglish is on the south side of the river where there is a good house and plantation of Mr Hingston. In the rebellion of 1641 Tiege MCormac MCarthy forfeited this estate (This Mr Hingston 298 is great grandson of the above 206). He was instituted in 1751 to the vicarage of Clonmen in the diocese of Cloyne, and in 1772 to the prebend of Donoghmore, and died in 1776. He was uncle to the learned and eminent Doctor Hales, formerly fellow of Trinity College, author of the Chronology H.

207 John Hingston

208 William Hingston (Lieut RN)

209 Benezer Murdock Hingston

(HN#9) Second son of James 206 and Katherine 2024 born 1750 was Captain in the British Army in the United States during the War for Independence. He married Precella (2025) daughter of William Compton Esq one of the Sheriffs of the State of Pennsylvania US and with her obtained considerable landed property but having been afterwards actively employed in the Kings army during the War of Independence he as a British Loyalist forfeited those possessions. His father in law William Compton is thus reported "A lot of Tories, who took part with Great Britain in the Revolutionary War and were attainted for High Treason, Commonly called the Black List" - Page 10. Compton William surrendered and discharged.

The above Back List is of those only in the State of Pennsylvania, and the said William Compton was Sheriff of the state under the Crown. His daughter Priscilla married Major Benezer Murdock Hingston of HM Service and for his adherence to the crown lost all of their larger property. The attainder was afterwards raised but the property was not returned. By Prescella his wife. Mr Hingston had five sons and three daughters. John (217), James (219), Spencer (218), William (220), Clayton (221), Catherine (246) & Mary (247). The name of the younger girl I do not know but I believe she died young. Major Hingston while in the army sold the estate of Aglish to his brother James 210 the third son of James 206 of Kilpaddie and died between 1804-10.

210 James Hingston LLD

(HN#18) Vicar General of the Diocese of Cloyne, Rector of Ahaballoge, and Justice of the Peace for the County of Cork was the third son of James 206 and Katherine 2024. he was born at Aglish 1755. he married Anne (2026) daughter of the Rev William Hodnett, Rector of Aghadown the same county and had issue James 222, William 223, Richard 224, Martha 226, Anne 227, Louisa 228, Isabella 354 (clearly added later, CJB). In the Topographer and Genealogist by John Gough Nicholas F.S.A. he says "Not one of Mr Hingston's Judicial decisions was ever reversed or set aside of appealed." I the writer have one of his Commissions given during the Triennial Visitation, Cloyne 1838 give by Richard Archbishop of Cashel and signed by James Hickey, Register.Mr Hingston died 6 Dec 1840 and was buried the 9th of the same month in the family vault outside the Cloyne Cathedral. there is also a slab within the building having the following inscription. "Sacred to the memory of the Rev James Hingston LLD Vicar General of the Diocese of Cloyne and Rector of the parish of Ahabologue, eminently distinguished during a long life for an unaffected piety, extensive benevolence, hospitality, no less than for classical and legal attainments, and universally admitted integrity and impartiality. He fell asleep in Jesus the sixth of December 1846 in the 85 Year of his Age by which he discharged the duties of Oeconomus to the Dean and Chapter for 40 Years, and the fact of no appeal having been made from his decisions as Judge of the Consistorial Court for half a Century, proves his accurate knowledge of Ecclesiastical Law, the Equity of his Mind; and the Soundness of his Judgement has been unparalleled" (This is not identical to the copy I made recently - CJB). Mrs Anne Hingston his wife died the 8th of Feb 1827 aged 74 years; their children Richard Thomas Hingston 87 RIF died of wounds in Spain 1809, The Rev William Hales Hingston died the 28th of Jan 1823. Mrs Catherine Ann Roger died the 24 March 1829. Mrs Martha Johnston died the 16th Dec 1831. Their grandchildren Thomas Hodnett Johnston died the 21 Jan 1824, James the son of the Rev Wm Hingston died the 20th Dec 1821. James Hingston Rogers KSL died the 29th of July 1840.

216 Isabella Hingston

Youngest daughter of James 206 and Katherine 2024 born 1769 married first George Brereton Esq 2030 of Co Carlow, and had two sons both deceased and secondly to Sir James Laurence Cotter Bart 2031 of Rockforrest near Mallow Sir James died 9 Feb 1829 leaving issue by Isabella James Lawrence 3018 the father of the present Sir J L Cotter also John R 3019, George E 3020, Richard B 3021 Henry J 3032, Nelson K 3023, Isabella 3024, Henrietta 3025, Catherine 3026 & Thomasen 3027. Isabella survived her husband four years and died April 1832.

217 John Hingston

Eldest son of Benezer 209 and Priscilla 2025. Married to Judith 2032 sister of Colonel Limerick EICS (East India Company Service - CJB) of Union Hall Co Cork. D.S.P.

218 Spencer Hingston

(HN#56) The second son of Benezer 209 and Priscilla 2025. Went to Australia and died there about 1870. Had three sons and three daughters. Clayton 262, James 263, William 264, Mary Ann 265, Margaret 266, Martha 267 all now in Australia.

219 James Hingston

(HN#50) Third son of Benezer 209 and Priscilla 2025 was Lieut Colonel commanding Royal African Corps and formerly of the 83rd Regiment in which he served during the Peninsular War, the Cape of Good Hope & Ceylon. He succeeded Sir Charles McCarthy as Governor of Cape Coast Castle (Ghana - CJB) and was wounded at the battle of "Indowah" Ashantee 1826 (probably the Battle of Dodowa, also known as Katamanso in the First Ashanti War in 1826 - CJB), while fighting at the head of his troops he received 32 wounds before he fell. He returned to England, was pensioned and married to Jane Mahany(??) 2154 and died at Gravesend England 1838.

220 William Hingston

The fourth son of Benezer 209 and Priscilla 2025 was an officer in the Royal Navy and died of yellow fever in the West Indies on board His Majesty's Ship "Cyane" (Probably in the period 1812-14 - CJB) never married.

221 Clayton Love Hingston

Youngest son of Benezer 209 and Priscilla 2025 born 14 April 1788 born 14 April 1788. Married his cousin Mary Ann 232 daughter of John 211 who died without issue. He married secondly Bedelia Fitzmaurice (234), daughter of Squire Fitzmaurice of Shoreham Esq and by her left three sons and two daughters all now (1903) living in America.; William J 233, Clayton L 234, John C 235, Priscilla S 236 & Catherine 237. Mr Hingston was born at Glendore and died at Peafield House near Bandon in 4 July 1871. He was appointed by the Duke of Wellington then Premier to be Comptroller of Customs for Westport and Newport Co Mahyo(sic).

222 James Hingston Rev

(HN#31) Of Whitechurch, son of James 210 and Ann 2026 was born 1784 and married 11 July 1812, Lucinda (2034) daughter of Richard Hedge Becher of Holybrook by his second wife Mary Alleyn of Cork. Her, that is Lucinda's, great grandfather Col. Thomas Becher was Aide-de-Camp to William III at the Battle of the Boyne on which occasion the King presented him with his watch, a valued relic, still in the possession of the Becher family. James by his wide Lucinda left issue James 238, Mary 239 & Lucinda 240 and died 23 Jan 1851.

223 William Hales Hingston

(HN#32) The second son of James 210 and Ann 2026 was born at Cloyne Co Cork 26 Jan 1790 was married 11 April 1812 to Anne (2035) daughter of the Rev George Sackville Cotter by his wife a daughter of Bailey Rogers. This made the second marriage between the two families for Mr Hingston's aunt Isabella Hingston had married Miss Cotter's uncle Sir James Lawrence Cotter. He was an author of no mean repute. Mr Hingston died 23 Jan 1823 leaving issue George SC 241, Margaret C 242, Annie M 243, Isabella 244, Arabella M 245 & James 353.

224 Richard Thomas Hingston

A son of James 210 and Ann 2026 was born at Cloyne 8 Jan 1772. Lieut in the 87th Regiment and killed at the Battle of Talavera in Spain on the 27 July 1809. At this battle the united British forces 19,000 and 30,000 of the Spanish under Arthur Wellesley (afterwards the Duke of Wellington) against the French Army 47,000 strong commanded by Marshals Victor & Sebastiani. The battle lasted two days with the victory for Wellington.

225 Helen Hingston

Of Old Castle daughter of William of Aglish 204 and Elizabeth 2023. Born at Aglish 1726. Married the Rev Sam Hales DD. Their son William Hales DD is the author of the new chronology of the Bible, see 3028. Helen died 3 March 1787.

226 Martha Hingston

Eldest daughter of James 210 and Anne 2026 was married 1 Oct 1796 to Joseph Johnstone Esq 2037 of Fort Johnstone Co Monaghan died 21 Jan 1824 and reinterred at Cloyne 19 Oct 1831.

227 Annie Catherine Hingston

228 Louisa Hingston

229 Arthur Bernard Hingston

Who died young was eldest son of John 211 and Alice 2027.

230 Francis Bernard Hingston

The second son of John 211 and Alice 2027 was Lieut in the 84th Regiment. His cousins Arthur Bernard was Major. Samuel Bemish was Captain and and Bernard Bemish was Lieut in the same Regiment and four other cousins Thomas; Vincent; George and Adderly Bemish were all officers in the Army. On Thursday the 29 May 1838 I find he presided as Worshipful Master of the Ancient Boyne Lodge No 84 of the Ancient Free and Accepted Masons, it being the Centennial anniversary of the foundation of that Lodge. The following officers were present. Francis Bernard Hingston, Worshipful Master; Adderly Bemish, Senior Warden; Franklin Baldwin, Junior Warden; Robert J Belcher, Junior Deacon; James Hamilton, Junior Deacon; William Belcher, Sect. and Treasurer; Richard Bailey, Senior Tyler; Hugh Douglas, Junior Tyler. Also 21 members of the 84th Regiment, 3 Grand Officers and 64 Visitors, total 96. Among the names on the muster roll of this venerable lodge will be found the following:- The Rev Sir Michael Cox; Sir John Dutilize; Sir Emanuel Moore; Sir Augustus Warren; George Earl of West-Meath; Francis Earl of Bandon; John Baron of Kinsale; the Hon James Butler; the Hon John Cunningham; the Hon William Dec-Coursey; General Garibaldi; Lieut Genl Sir Erye Coote; Colonel Boothby; Colonel Hall; Colonel King; Lieut Col. Blake; Capt Blonden 18th Light Dr; Capt Cane 12th Light Dr; Capt Dyson 3 Dr Guards; Capt Hndley 6 Dr Guards; Capt Quintin 10 Hussars; Major Williams Light Dr; Capt Younghusband Dr Guards & Charles Kendel Brake. William F B Hingston died at Omagh in the north of Ireland in 1866.

231 James Bernard Hingston

Youngest son of John 211 and Alice 2027 married Miss Hull 2223 of Macroom, sister to Mr Hull who married Lucinda Hingston and had by her nine children John 250, Francis 251, Arthur 252, Charles 253, James 254, Augustus 255, Alicia 256, Elizabeth 257 & Mary 258 further than the above I know not. (There may be some confusion here JBH is the same as HN#70 with similar children, but we show Maria Hull as the wife of HN#59 James - this needs to be checked - CJB)

233 William James Hingston

Eldest son of Clayton Love Hingston 221 of Glendore by his wife Bedelia 2033 born 8 Feb 1849. Went to New York and there married Miss Rebecca Johnson 2148 on the 6 Jan 1886. She died 24 July 1899 having no issue. Mr W J Hingston has had a chequered life, involved in many ventures and made a number of inventions and is at the present time (1903) engaged in photography in New York.

234 Clayton Love Hingston

Second son of Clayton 221 and Bedelia 2033 born at Glendore 14 April 1852. Graduated in Glasgow as a Veterinary Surgeon and served two years in the 3rd US Cavalry and is now practicing in Boston (1903) married the 9 Sept 1885 to Miss Katherine Ann Ford 2152 of Sligo and has two daughters, Mary Helen (260) and Ethel Louisa (261).

235 John Compton Hingston MRCVS

Third son of of Clayton 221 and Bedelia 2033 born at Glendore 18 Oct 1853 graduated at Glasgow practiced there and in the north of England married to Miss Helen Jane Dimond 2040 of Glasgow the 12 April 1877 and in 1880 came to America and settled in Philadelphia; in 1884 he moved to Boston and then to New York where he had a large practice. Mr J C Hingston is a man peculiarly adapted(??) for his profession. His judgement as to the ailments of a horse is infallible and as soon as he comes in contact with an animal then is a friendship established that materially assists him in their cure. He is known among the profession as "a marvelous man with a horse". When last heard from was in the southern states.

236 Precella Selina Hingston

The eldest daughter of Clayton 221 and Bedelia 2033 born at Glendore 28 Aug 1856 and after the death of her father came with her mother and sister to join her brothers in America on the 19 August 1884. She married George S Ramsden 2153 of Leicestershire and by him has one son George. Mr Ramsden was killed by the Cars in Chicago 21 Aug 1887. Pricilla married again in Oct 1888 the Rev John McGain of Philadelphia; he died 25 April 1890 without issue.

241 George Sackville Cotter Hingston

(HN#35) Rector of Queenstown born at Cloyne 1816 the second son of William 223 and Annie 2035 married in 1840 Miss Isabella Rudkin (2237) of Corris(??) who in her youth was spoken of as the Infant "Lyra" she died 28 Oct 1888. 243 Annie Matilda Isabella Hingston

Daughter of William 223 and Annie 2035 born June 1815 at Cloyne.

244 Isabella Charlotte Hingston

Daughter of William 223 and Annie 2035 born 15 Nov 1818 lived for the most time before her marriage with her grandfather James Hingston 210 LLD married 23 Feb 1854 to Joseph C Wakeham of Midleton who died 1 Oct 1871 leaving all the property and their house "Carriganiska", all of which had come to him with his wife to his nephew J Wakeham. Mrs Wakeham survived her husband 29 years and died the 17 July 1900 at her home. She was an author and musician among her published books are "The watcher", "The Ice Broken", "The Good Pleasure", "Harold Summer's Joy", "Far off made high", "The Pass", "A contrast" etc etc. Her last written in 1898 (her 80th year) "Helen's Trial" is not in print yet. She loved music and would sit for hours playing; she found it great comfort in her last years and her touch was like that of a young girl and it was almost impossible to keep ones feet still when she played a dancing tune. She left no issue.

248 Clayton S H Hingston

Regt in memory of five officers and three ladies is the name of Selina Maria wife of Capt C.S.Hingston 3 W.I. Regt died at Up-Park Camp 11 April 1854, aet 23" also in the new Burial Ground at Up-Park Camp grave No 12 "Selina Hingston and Louisa her infant aged 3 months". 249 Clayton William James Hingston

Son of Clayton 248 and Selina 2043 born 1852 in the West Indies. Married Miss Grey 2159, daughter of the Rev Dr Grey of Edinburgh, Scotland was commissioned 22 Jan 1868 and while an Ensigne he visited his father's cousin Clayton Love 221 at Peafield House in 1871 was promoted Lieut. 28 Oct 1871, Captain 22 Jan 1880, Major 22 Jan 1888, Lieut. Col. 22 Jan 1894. Has had 27 years service in the 62nd Foot and 8 years Colonel command the 10th Bengal Infantry. He served with the Burmese Expedition in 1888-9 was mentioned in despatches received a medal with clasps and with the Chin-Lushai Expeditionary Force in 1889-90 (clasp). Colonel Hingston is still in command this Spring (1903).

286 Richard Hingston

Youngest son of George 241 and Isabella 2237 born at Queenstown 14 Feb 1855. Married Miss Annie L Cottom 2803 in 1888 and went to Sydney New South Wales and has five children Richard 638, Ivy 639, William 640, Arthur 641 and Edith 642. Mr Hingston was thrown from his horse and struck on the top of his head about six years ago (1896) which has resulted in his total blindness at this writing (1903) in 1902. He with his family returned to England and are now on a small farm near London in Essex Co. The following are a few lines he wrote upon his sight failing him.
Lines written on failing eyesight // E'en tho' the shadow nears day by day / And steals apace to veil those eyes that know / A world so fair with violets mid their due / The laughing sweetness of the breath of May // And hide each bloom along my rugged way / That let my path beneath the closing blue / Wherein no star may shine to lead me thro' / Or guide my slumberous wandrings lest they stray // A chastened murmur thro' the stillness rings / In rays divine, and visions that may be / To brighten on thro' darkness still it beams / Aye clear and clearer for the shade that stings / Thro' those wan hours by Times long rolling Sea / Sound chords that tend to light beyond these dreams.

355 Milesom Hingston

Of London. Bookseller son of Milesom 309 and Elizabeth 2310 born 1738 and married in 1786 to Miss Ann Winter. The Booksellers Sale Catalogue quoting from Nicholas's List of Anecdotes Vol 3, P645 says "Hingeston Mileson. Strand, near Temple Bar. After being several years in business he retired to a comfortable situation in the Ordnance Office, and died much respected at his house in the Tower, March 24 1806." and in the European Magazine, Vol 49, May 1806 P 402 it says "Died April 24, Mr Mileson Hingston aged 68, formerly a Bookseller in the Strand, but lately having a place in the Office of Ordnance" and on a flat stone upon the terrace below the Small Armoury in the Tower was the following inscription. "Here lieth the remains of Mr Milsom Hingeston, who died March 24 1806 aged 68 years. Clerk in His Majesties Ordnance Office 24 years. Also Mrs Ann Hingeston wife of the above who died Jan 28 1793 aged 33 years. Likewise Robert Winter Hingeston (311) who died Sept 12th 1789 aged 5 months, also Ann Hingeston (312) daughter of the aforesaid, who died May 19th 1796 aged 3 years and 4 months." I the writer of this visited the Tower with a permit from Colonel Commanding and one of the warders took me to the Terrace showed me where the Small Armoury once stood. (It was burned down on 1840) and also showed me where the grave and stones were moved to, some 600 feet to the west and along the side of the Chapel. The stone I was looking for is at the south east corner of the Chapel.

371 John Hingston

Of Truro and St Ives. Eldest son of John 370 and Mary 2150 entered the Navy when quite young and was killed at the Battle of Trafalgar 21 Oct 1805.

372 Francis Hingston

Second son of John 370 and Mary 2150 born 27 Nov 1796 was married to Miss Jane Matilda Wilson (2142) of Kirkness about 1830 succeeded his father as Collector of H.M. Customs at Truro and St Ives is the author of a book of poems edited by his son and published in 1857. His home at Truro was called "Truro Vean Cottage" a very good engraving of which is used for the frontispiece of the book of poems. I quote from the Introduction by the Editor "To show how little he cared for fame "Of myself" he writes in answer to the enquiries of the Historian of Cornwall the Rev Richard Polwhele "one word is enough. I was educated at the Truro Grammar School and I had for my school-fellows your two eldest sons, now, I believe, both serving in India. As to my literary productions, they consist chiefly of the few pieces already in your hands, which have been preserved wholly by accident, and some other trifles in prose and verse, which were not worth preserving. They were written only to amuse our idle hour, not beguile a weary one"

Thou Glorious Sea / Here Glorious Sea! More pleasing for / When all thy waters are at rest / and noon-day sun or midnight star / is shining on thy worthless(??) breast // More pleasing far than when the wings / of stormy winds an oer thee spread / and every billowy mountain flings / aloft to Heaven its foaming head. // (??) is the very tempest dear, / Whose mighty voice but tells of thee; / For wil(??), or colour - or far, or near / I love the still, thou glorious sea!

Inscription of an alcove in a Friends garden / Hush softly tread this lowly shade / Where holy solace loves to dwell - / now let the sounds of mirth invade / the stillness of her lonely cell! // 'Tis hers to give that sweet repose, / To every child of sorrow dear; / and he who's wounded spirit knows / no healing balm may find it here!

The above two piece are a fair sample of his poems. He was very fond of St Ives where he had spent most of his life and in his last illness he desired to be taken there; where he died the 7 Oct 1841 his wife survived him 45 years.

373 Thomas Hingston MD

The third and youngest son of John 370 and Mary 2150 born at St Ives 9 May 1799 was educated in his native town and at Queens' College Cambridge. His medical studies commenced in the house of a general practitioner and having availed(??) himself of the opportunities of an extensive practice, which the connection afforded him. He removed to Edinburgh in the year 1821. In 1822 he won the gold medal offered by George IV to Edinburgh University for a Latin ode on the occasion of his visit to Scotland (see below). In 1824 he was admitted to the degree of M.D. after publishing an inaugural dissertation 'De Morbo Comitiale' and in the same year he brought out a new edition of William Harvey's "De Mostic Cordis et Sanguinis" with additions and corrections. Dr Hingston first practiced as a physician at Penzance 1828-32 and afterwards moved to Truro. He contributed to the Transactions of the Geological Society of Cornwall a dissertation "On the use of iron among the 'Earlier Nations' of Europe" Vol IV 113-134 and to Vol IV of Davies Gilbert's "Parochial History of Cornwall" he furnished a "Memoir of William of Worcester" and an essay "On the Etymology of Cornish Names" He died at Falmouth whither he had removed for the benefit of the sea air the 13 July 1837. This translation was made by Francis Hingston his brother in September 1822.

"Ode // To commemorate the King's visit to Scotland // O thou whose favour'd sceptre Heaven defend / To whose wild rule a mighty Empire bends, / Whose matchless glory, like thy boundless sway, / Outruns the light, and still salutes the day. / O'er other realms the sun may set or shine, / It glows for ever on some land of thine! // Her (??) the for nations own their Line, their King; / To thee come blessings on the Zephyr's wing; / and India's son, what'er his faith may be, / Prays to his prophet or his Stream for thee // How great, how pure the ecstacies that rise / When thy proud banner greets our northern skies / While joyous thunders from Edina's crest / Disturb with welcome noise the Muse's rest. // Now Scotland bounds with bliss untasted long, / and rouses all her mountain-tops to song; / She bids her town rejoice, her valleys sing. / and her glad children flock to hail their King. // To thee our hearts, our voices all are given, / For thee nations shout ascends to Heaven, / O mighty Prince! Thy grateful subjects own / My gentle sway is fix'd in love alone; / and fondly round thy honour'd throne repair; / and bend and offer faithful homage there! // Illustrious Monarch! though we spend our years / Within the peaceful dome that learning rears, / Safe in thy might, and in thy fever blest, / We raise the modest flowers that place the best. // Strangers to War, yet should the trumpet's breath / Call forth thy subjects to the field of death, / At thy command; or ere thou great command, / First in the ranks of honour would we stand. / But happier tack awaits us; now we raise / Amid our lofty halls the song of praise; / The flying breeze, swift succunger(??) of fame, / bends from its high career(??) to catch thy name! / And still - while these eternal portals rise / said(??) etherial light and and azure skies - / Still shall their roofs resound the exulting lay / that bids the welcome on our shores today. // Long Sovereign of the Free! May'st thou retain / The towering glories of they splendid reign; / Long rule, as thou hast ruled, with gentle hand, / The ancient sites of our buried land; / Till having (????) causes thine eyes shall see / a realm more glorious still reserved for thee / May see as thou thy earthly charge lay'st down / The spotless throne - the (????) thine crown" Thomas Hingeston

"On the picture of a mother, wounded and dying, nursing her child // From the Greek // "Take, hapless child, not long the power will be; / Take the last drop that bosom has for thee!" / She bleeds and sinks, nor fails in death to prove, / How triumphs thee a Mother's living love." J.H.

374 Frances Charles Hingston Rev

Son of Frances 372 and Jane 2142 born at St Ives Cornwall 31 March 1833 was educated at Truro Grammar School and at Exeter College Oxford (Matr 12 Nov 1851, Scholar 1850, BA 1855, MA 1858.) Having held a curacy in Oxford (Holywell), he was appointed in 1859 to the Perpetual Curacy of Hampton Gay, near Oxford and in 1860 to the Rectory of Ringmore, Devon. He was appointed Domestic Chaplain to the Baroness Despencer (Dowager Viscountess Falmouth) 1859; Rural Dean 1879 and Prebendary of Exeter 1885. He is the author of "Specimens of Ancient Cornish Crosses, Fonts, etc", 1850; "Four years of a Country Friendly Society", 1870; has edited "The poems of Francis Hingston" 1857; "The chronicle of England by John Capgrave" (under the direction of the Master of the Rolls); "Johannis Capgravie, Liber de Illustribus Henricis. (in the same series); "The Book of the Illustrious Henries" (translated from the Latin of Capgrave) 1858 and "A collection of Royal and Historical letters during the Reign of Henry IV" (For the Master of the Rolls) 1860; "The Register of Edmund Stafford Bishop of Exeter", 1886; "The Registers of Walter Bronescombe and Peter Quivil, Bishops of Exeter", 1889 and a great number of other works since then. Mr Hingston (Hingeston crossed out - CJB) married in 1860 Martha Jane (2143), only child of the Rev Herbert Randolph MA of Ringmore whose name he assumed and now writes his name Francis Charles Hingeston-Randolph. (Note elsewhere she is referred to as Matilda - CJB) He lives at the Rectory a fine modern house surrounded by beautiful grounds. The living of Ringmore is a rectory, with the Chapel of Okenbury, net yearly value of £306 including 101 acres of Glebe. The church of All Saints about 40 rods to the east is an ancient building chiefly of the latter part of the 13 Century, but the North Transept is of early Norman or possibly Saxon date. The church was restored in the years 1861-64 when upon removing the seven coats of whitewash three copies of the ten commandments and two of the Lords prayer were discovered painted one upon the top of the other and below all this was discovered a fine sample of fresco in terracotta colours which Mr Hingston preserved, but some portion had been destroyed before it was noticed. Mr Hingston had that restored but though done by the best workmen in London the restored part has faded several shades when the original which is at the very least over 300 years old is as bright as ever. He also added several stained glass windows in memory of some of the Hingston family. Mr and Mrs Hingston have raise a large family 5 boys and 6 girls. Francis 376, Herbert 378, Bernard 379, Herbert 380, Charles 384, Jane 377, Rose 381, Margaret 382, Katherine 383, Grace 385 and Barbara 386.

390 Henry Hingston Rev

Of Kingsbridge, son of William 389 and Sarah 2634 born 1668 and died 1724. Mr Hingston made quite a stir when in 1703 he published his book "Dreadful Alarm upon the Clouds of Heaven". For many years the writer tried to get a copy and at last found one in an old book store and strange to say on the inside cover found the following memo, apparently written many years ago "Son of William and Sarah H of Kingsbridge, Died 1724 aged 56". The book is in three parts. The first is an address to England containing sundry warning and admonitions to the inhabitants thereof, in which the sundry pastimes of the age gets a good rating especially football. The second is a Vindication of an often celebrated and confirmed vision concerning the worship of the Church of England. The third is an expostulation directed to the Arch-Bishops, Bishops Dignified and Inferior Clergy of the Church of England. The whole is both dedicated to and Prostrated before the Queen (Queen Anne). Printed at Exeter by Sam Farley 1703. I believe he died unmarried.

493 Thomas Hingston

The eldest son of Richard 491 by his second wife Catherine 2415 born at Cincinnati 30 Nov 1847. He insists on spelling the name "Hinston" and says that his father said that was the way. But the compiler of these records, while visiting the son Richard and in the presence of four or five of the family was shown an old chest belonging to the father and upon removing the dust the name was found painted on the top "Richard Hingston". Mr Hingston is now farming in Indiana I believe.

501 William Hingston

(HN#79) William the first in this branch was the third son of William 204 of Aglish and Elizabeth 2023. Born 1700. Was one of five sons, the elder James received the Estate of "Aglish", the other four got each £1000. (NB - This is not consistent with WEH's own entry for William 204, which only mentions two sons - CJB) Mr Hingston married about 1725 Miss Fannie Bevins, it is supposed in South Devon. In 1729 he settled at Whitehall near Cape Clear and went into the mackerel fishery and coast trading vessels. He left three sons and two daughters. Edward 502, Samuel 503, Alan 515, Fannie 516 and Charlotte 517.

502 Edward Hingston

(HN#30) Son of William 501 born at Whitehall 1733 became Lieut. in the Royal Navy and served under Vice Admiral Edward Thornborough on board the "Trent". He is said to have made at least one voyage to find the "North West Passage" under Sir John Franklin. At any rate Hingston Bay in Greenland (Lat 74 Deg N and Long 56 W) is called after him. He married Miss Lucretia Sewell 2046 niece of Rear Admiral Charles Holmes in 1770 at Deptford England and died 10 Mar 1813 on board ship in Cork Harbour. His son William was appointed in his room 29 April 1813. Ten of his children survived him namely Lucretia 505; Fannie 506; Dorah 507; William 508; Allan 509; Betsey 510; Mary 511; Ann 512; Samuel 513 and Jane 514. (Note that the story here about Franklin is improbable - Franklin's first voyage to the Arctic was not until 1818 - after Edward had died. It is more likely that it was the son William who went with Franklin - CJB)

503 Samuel Hingston

(HN#80) Son of William 501 and Francis 2045 born 1726. There is some doubt about this date for the descendants of Samuel claim he was born at Whitehall in 1726 when it is a fact that the father William did not come to that part of the country until 1729. (I believe 1726 to be the date of the marriage and 1735 to be the date of birth because of other births. WEH) Mr Hingston in 1750? married Ann Smyth 2047. By this marriage both the names of Freke and Townsend came into the Hingston family and also the first connection with the family of La Touche of Dublin. Mr Hingston settled in Whitehall and developed the mackerel fishery which his descendants still follow. He died in 1813 and left issue William 519 and Freke 518. N.B. The name Freke formerly spelled Freki is the name of one of the wolves of Odin and Anglo-Saxon God of War.

504 Ann Margaret Hingston

Eldest child of Edward 502 and Lucretia 2046. Died young.

505 Lucretia Hingston

Second daughter of Edward 502 and Lucretia 2046 born Tuesday 10 Dec 1772 and married Mr Bennett 2048.

506 Fannie Hingston

Third daughter of Edward 502 and Lucretia 2046 born 19 March 1774 married to James Baker 2049 of Baltimore and had five children. Two sons James 3034 and Isaac 3036 and three daughters Mary Hingston 3035, Lucretia 3037 and Fannie 3038.

507 Dorah Hingston

Fourth daughter of Edward 502 and Lucretia 2046 born Tuesday 10 Aug 1777. Married to John Abbott of Aggadowne and had two children. Joseph Allan 3339 and Anne 3382 (see notes on the Abbott family).

508 William Hingston

(HN#21) Eldest son of Edward 502 and Lucretia 2046 born Sunday 6 Sept 1778. Joined His Majesties signal service 21 Sept 1805 at Cape Clear, Ireland. Married Jane Carroll 2051 of Old Court at Whitehall 31 March 1807 was appointed as Midshipman in room of his father on board of the warship "Trent" 29 April 1813. (If this story is correct, Edward would have been 80 when he left the Trent and William would have been 35 when he started as a midshipman, both of which seem improbable - midshipmen were usually teenagers in training to be officers - CJB) His father dieing the 10 May following. While midshipman on board the Trent he made a trip or voyage with Lieut Franklin (afterwards Sir John Franklin) in company with the "Dorothy" in search of the "North west Passage" and returned to Deptford 22 Oct 1818. Joined the Preventative Coast Guard at Cape Clear 27 Sept 1820 and was commissioned Lieut 13 Feb 1822. Family moved from Cape Clear to Baltimore 1826 from Baltimore to Whitehall (some times called Ringcove or Rincoe) in 1829. He joined the Revenue at Wexford 10 Feb 1833 and was superannuated on the 15 July 1847 after being in the service 42 years. He left Ireland and joined his sons William 525 and John 526 in Buffalo NY the 12 Aug 1851 and died in Buffalo at 9pm 1 Dec 1854 was buried in the old grave yard on High Street. His bones were disinterred and buried again on the funeral of his great grandson Raymond C 599 on the 22 Feb 1891 at Forest Lawn Cemetery in Lot 599 section 10 belonging to his grandson William 540. At this burial of Mr William Hingston there were present one son, two grandsons; and one great grandson all of the name William. Mr Hingston is reported to have had the remarkable gift or power of stopping bleeding by simply laying his hands upon the person near the wound. He was the father of Edward 520, Mary 521, Lucretia 522, Ann 523, Jane 524, William 526, Samuel 527 and Eliza 528.

509 Alan Hingston

(HN#33) Second son and sixth child of Edward 502 and Lucretia 2046 born at Whitehall Co Cork Saturday 21 Dec 1779. Married to Hannah Bennett 2052 and is the father of Richard 529, William 530, Alan 531 and Freke 532.

She died in 1874 and is buried at Rochester NY.

512 Ann Hingston

He came to America 15 April 1822 settled first at the St Laurence River afterwards at Rochester and finally at Buffalo, died 13 April 1861 and left issue Edward 533, John 534, Lucretia 535, Kate 536 and Mary 537.
