518 Freke Hingston

(HN#81) Of Whitehall, son of Samuel 503 and Ann 2047 born 1764, married Kate Vickery 2049 and died 7 April 1853, having issue Samuel 538 and John 539. Mr Freke Hingston who devoted his time to the mackerel fishery and his farms bought out the interests of Lieut Edward Hingston 502 who had a large family of daughters and became sole owner of the estate of Whitehall which has now passed into the hands of the Townsends of Castle Townsend.

519 William Hingston

Eldest son of Samuel 503 and Ann 2047 born 1755 and died 25 Nov 1852 without issue. Mr Hingston was always known and spoken of as "Old Commodore". He never did anything in the shape of work in his life just walked around and enjoyed himself in a quiet leisurely fashion and watched the fleet of fishing boats as they came in loaded with their beautiful catch of Spanish Mackerel caught between the south west coast of Ireland and the Scilly Islands. I presume it was the fishermen who christened him the "Commodore" as he was the owner of most of the boats. These boats were large handsome trawlers and carried each a crew of seven men, were built at Baltimore and at the Isle of Man were very strong and safe as they had to be for those waters.

520 Edward Hingston

(HN#25) Eldest son of William 508 and Jane 2051 born at Whitehall now called Rincoe Sunday 7am 25 June 1809. Moved to Cape Clear in 1814 and to Baltimore in 1816. At the latter place learned the shipbuilding. Moved from there to Wexford in 1829 and commenced business for himself. Built the cutter "Victoria", the schooners "Lucinda Jane", "Atlas", "Dolphin", "Prince Albert", "Princes", "Handy" and the brig "Nature". Also a number of race boats, gigs etc, noted for their speed, among them the "Wexford Girl" and the "Munster Lass". Two or three of the schooners and the brig are still in Commission (1903) and over 60 years old. Mr Hingston moved from Wexford to Kingstown near Dublin January 1841 and there built the Brig the "Duchess of Leinster", the cutter "Kate" and others. Was married to Elizabeth Armstrong 2060 eldest daughter of John Jenkins by his wife Ann Creighton of Whitehaven Co Cumberland England at Monkstown Church under special licence the 2nd day of Sep 1841. Mr Hingston launched the "Dutchess of Leinster" 25 March 1843 there were present at the launch, The Right Honourable the Lord Mayor and Lady Mayoress of Dublin; the Duke and Dutchess of Leinster; the Marquess of Kildare; Lords William and Otho Fitzgerald, Lady Beaujolais Bury, Sir John Kingston James and many others of note. Before the ceremony the Dutchess of Leinster presented Mrs Hingston with a very handsome dress for her baby then eight months old (William 540). through the failure of Mr Fagan the timber merchant of Dublin for whome the vessel was built Mr Hingston lost heavily and having made up his mind to go to America refused to take charge of the Government shipyards at Cardiff. Left Kingstown with his family and three brothers William 525, John 526 and Samuel 527 for Liverpool 21 July 1843. Sailed from Liverpool on board the "Xylon" for New York 26 July and arrived in New York 8 Sept 1843 and Thomastown Thursday 7 Oct where he commenced business, and had just started to build a vessel when he was taken down with a malignant fever and pneumonia after a few days he died the 4 Jan 1844. Some 18 days before his second son was born. He is buried in Thomastown (now Rockland) State of Maine. His wife returned to England with her two children in March the same year (see No 2060).

521 Mary Ann Hingston

Eldest daughter of William 508 and Jane 2051 born at Whitehall Thursday 4am 9 May 1811. Died unmarried

522 Lucretia Hingston

Second daughter of William 508 and Jane 2051 born 4am 19 Feb 1813 was considered an exceedingly handsome woman died unmarried at Wexford 23 Sep 1838 in the 26 year of her age.

523 Ann Hingston

Third daughter of William 508 and Jane 2051 born at Cape Clear 7am Thursday 7 Nov 1815 was married to the Rev. John Clarke of Wexford the 4 January 1837. Sailed for America 7 June 1839 arrived in New York 19 Aug; their second child Lucretia 3055 was born that same night. Her husband died leaving her with five children. She married secondly William Woods of Boston and had two sons and died 10th January 1871 at Lynn Mass. Her children were Richard 3054, Lucretia 3055, Eliza 3056, Adam 3057, Louisa 3058 and William 3059 and John 3060.

524 Jane Hingston

Fourth daughter of William 508 and Jane 2051 born Sunday 1 am 30 Nov 1817 married Peter Bryne and died at Wexford 4 Jan 1847 leaving one child Peter 3052.

525 William Hingston

(HN#22) Second son of William 508 and Jane 2051 born in Cape Clear Lighthouse at 2am Tuesday 11 Jan 1820. He learned the trade of shipbuilding at Wexford and Kingstown under his brother Edward 520 and with him came to America in 1843. Was godfather to W.E.Hingston 540 (the author of these notes) and took care of him on board ship. He with the assistance of John Cuthbert another apprentice made his brothers coffin 5 Jan 1844. The next year he returned to England was employed for a time in the Woolwich Dock Yards, and again came to America in 1847. Was married in Lynn Mass 2 May 1850 to Charlotte Cummins 2064 and moved to Buffalo NY where he settled and followed the business of boatbuilding in company with his brother John under the firm name "Hingston Bros". Since the death of his brother the firm has been known as "Mr Hingston and Son". They built the boat for the Niagara Falls called the "Maid of the Mist" in 1892 and launched it from the top of the bank. His wife died 13 Jan 1877. Their children were Cuthbert 542, Caroline 543, Charlotte 544, William 545, Lucretia 546 and Jane 547. Mr Hingston died Monday 11pm the 9 April 1900 in the 81 year of his age and buried in the family lot in "Forest Lawn Cemetery" mourned by a large number of his friends and relations.

526 John Townsend Hingston

(HN#24) Third son of William 508 and Jane 2051 born Sunday 5am 2 June 1822 at Whitehall. He learned the shipbuilding trade with his brother Edward 520 and with him came to America in 1843. In the fall of 1844 after the death of his brother he returned to England and worked in the Government Dock Yards at Deptford and Pembrook. In 1847 he again came to America and settled in Buffalo was for a number of years and until his death in partnership with his brother William under the name of "Hingston Bros". On the 28 July 1849 was married to Miss Charlotte Bird 2065 of Kingstown at Buffalo by the Rev Dr Shelton and by her who still survives him (1903) he had one son William 548 and two daughters Juliana 549 and Emma 550. Also two other children who died young. On the 26 August 1879 while repairing a yawl boat laying in Buffalo Creek opposite the foot of Main St the boat gave a lurch causing him to strike himself in the forehead and stunning himself he fell into the water and was drowned before a large number of business people some of whom shouted out they would give large sums to have him saved but owing to delay caused by the passing shipping he was not rescued until life was extinct. He was buried at Forest Lawn Cemetery 28 Aug 1879 in lot 79 section B.

527 Samuel Hingston

(HN#82) The fourth and youngest son of William 508 and Jane 2051 a twin with his sister Eliza born at Cape Clear at 10am Saturday 20 August 1825. He with his other brothers learned the shipbuilding under the eldest Edward and also with him came to America in 1843. Coasted between New York and Thomastown Maine in the summer of 1844. Settled in Rochester NY in 1846 and there married Ann Anderson 2066 of Rack Island Co Cork. 31 March 1847 and by her had four children Ann 551, Jane 552, Richard 553 and Samuel 554. He died at Rochester NY aged 66 surrounded by his family on the 22 June 1891 of cancer of the lip.

528 Eliza Hingston

Youngest daughter of William 508 and Jane 2051. Twin with her brother Samuel; born at Cape Clear Co Cork at 10 am Saturday 20 Aug 1825. Though taller than the average woman when grown up she only weighed 2 1/2 pounds at birth and after being dressed by one of the family (the nurse would not touch her saying she was a fairy) she was presented to her father in one of his own slippers. She came to America August 1848 and was married in Rochester 28 April 1853 to Robert James Iberius Cooper 2067 of Beg-om(??) Island Co Wexford Ireland. Moved to Buffalo and settled there. Is the mother of Bessie 3062, Jane 3063, Henry 3064, John 3065 Samuel 3066 and Jacob(??) 3067. About 1878 moved to Mayville on Chataque Lake. She died survived by all her family and mourned by a large number of friends to whome she was always "Aunt Eliza" on the 1 May 1889 aged 64. N.B. One cannot help but notice upon looking over the records of this particular branch of the family that there are but few deaths among the children and they only by such malignant diseases as Diphtheria and Spinal Meningitis. The last ten deaths in this family read 89, 97, 35, 25, 56, 30, 80, 57, 66 & 59 averaging 59 and none of their children died sooner and the present average age of the thirteen now living in New York State is 50+ years.

529 Richard Hingston

(HN#83) Of Skibbereen son of Alan 509 and Hannah 2052 born 1814 and died of cholera in St Johns NB 1847. He left issue by his wife Eliza Coperthorn (2068) a son and daughter Alan 623 and Mary Ann 624. Mr Hingston is buried on Patricks Island.

530 William Hingston

(HN#37) Second son of Alan 509 and Hannah 2052 born 1816 married Ellen Wolf 2069 in 1851 and had by her five sons and one daughter Alan 555; Ann 556; William 557; Richard 558, Freke 559 & Joseph 560. He died 8 April 1882.

531 Alan Hingston

(HN#40) Third son of Alan 509 and Hannah 2052 born 1817 and married in 1850 to Bessie Driscoe 2070 who died in Oct 1865 leaving him seven children. Mary 561; Lizzie 562; Lucretia 563; Hannah 564; Charlotte 565; William 566 & Annie 567. He married secondly Eliza Kelly 2071 the 16 Aug 1868 who bore him seven other children. Richard 615; Allen 616; Susan 617; Freke 618; Samuel 619; Catherine 620 & Robert 621. His second wife and all but one of his children survive him Oct 1903. Mr Hingston died 15 Nov 1888. Mrs Hingston still lives upon the farm which is worked by her son Richard. The rest of the family being scattered over England, Canada and the United States.

532 Freke Hingston

(HN#39) The youngest son of Alan 509 and Hannah 2052 born 1822 and married Ann Kenny (2072) and by her who is still living on the farm at Whitehall he left eight children. Richard 568; Ann 569; Alan 570; Freke 571; William 572; Catherine 573; Ann 574 and Mathew 575. His family, like his brother's (Alan 531) is scattered broadcast but mostly in the neighbourhood of Boston Mass.

533 Edward Hingston

(HN#84) Eldest son of Samuel 513 and Catherine 2055 born at Skibbereen Dec 1816 married in 1838 to Sarah Justice 2073 daughter of Edward Justice of Four-mile-water and by her had two sons and seven daughters. Kate 576; Sarah 577; Samuel 578; Edward 579; Mary 580; Isabella 581; Mary E 582; Ida 583 & Emma 584. Mr Hingston came to America in 1822 with his father, learnt the shipbuilding trade in Brockville on the St Lawrence and Rochester NY settled in Buffalo about 1844 where he died 26 Feb 1902.

534 John Hingston

Second son of Samuel 513 and Catherine 2055 born at Skibbereen 2 May 1819. Learnt the shipbuilding trade but gave it up for farming. Went west married Miss Jane Brown 2074 and is now (1903) living on his farm near Minneapolis Minn. without issue.

535 Lucretia Hingston

Eldest child of Samuel 513 and Catherine 2055 born at Skibbereen 1814. Married in Rochester to William Franpir (??) 2075 and by him had ten children Jennie 3068; Louisa 3069; Eliza 3070; Anne 3071; Kate 3072; Samuel 3073; John 3074; David 3075; William 3076 and Mary 3077 all born in Rochester. Mrs Framper died 19 Oct 1895 aged 81 years.

536 Kate Hingston

Second daughter of Samuel 513 and Catherine 2055 born at Rochester 25 Dec 1822 and married 29 April 1841 to Richard Bemish of Cork has had five children Rachel 3078; Samuel 3079; Caroline 3080; Francis 3081 & Mary 3082. Mrs Bemish and three of the children are still living in Buffalo, Mr Bemish having died in 1871.

537 Mary Ann Hingston

Youngest child of Samuel 513 and Catherine 2055 born at Montreal Canada 20 May 182? married Robert McCombe of Armanat(??) 8 June 1848 and has six children William 3083; Kate 3084; Ann 3085; Samuel 3086; George 3087 & Robert 3088. All the family are now living and settled in Buffalo NY.

538 Samuel Hingston

(HN#34) Son of Freke 518 and Kate 2059 born at Whitehall 10 Feb 1818. Married first Ann Symms 2079 in 1848 she died 31 May 1873 without issue. Mr Hingston married secondly Eliza Wolf 2078 the 14 Sept 1876 and by her who now (1903) is living on one of the farms at Lasheen Co Cork five sons John 585; Joseph 586; Samuel 587; James 628 & Freke 629. The last of the estate, and the homestead of Whitehall was sold by Mrs Hingston as executor for the children in 1894. Mr Hingston died 20 Dec 1888.

539 John Hingston

(HN#38) Son of Freke 518 and Kate 2059 born at Whitehall 31 May 1822 was appointed to the Linen Draper trade in Skibbereen left it and served his time to the ship building with his cousin Edward 520. Spent three years in St Johns NB returned to Ireland and was for thirteen years agent on the Great Southern and Western Railway, and for thirty four years Chief Steward of Trinity College Dublin, also has been land agent the last 20 years for several estates. In Nov 1880 he received a threatening letter with the picture of a coffin and a double barreled gun drawn upon it telling him to "beware of the fate of Lord Mountmorris; that as clever a man, and as bright a man as he was they would be a match for him." But being on good terms with all the tenants, some 248 in all, occupying about 11,000 acres in Cork, Monaghan, Roscommon and Wicklow, he concluded not to scare, and lived until the 22 June 1893, when he died of gout, and is buried in Jerome Cemetery, Dublin. He had married Miss Elizabeth Botton 2080 on 10 September 1847, and had issue,- William 588, Emma 589, Samuel 590, John 591, Kate 592, and Arthur 593.

540 William Edward Hingston (author of these notes)

(HN#26) The eldest son of Edward 520 and Elizabeth 2060. Born at Kingstown, County Dublin, Ireland, 28 July 1842, was taken to America July, 1843. His father dying 4 January 1844, at Thomastown, Main, his mother returned in April, 1844, with him and his infant brother, to Whitehaven, England. Attended school (St. Mary's) in Liverpool, and served his time book-keeping in that city. Enllisted in the 28th Regiment of Foot, 8 May 1860; obtained his discharge at the close of the Indian Mutiny, and emigrated to America 18 June 1862. Learned the trade of shipbuilding with his uncles, Hingston & Bros. went South during the war of the Rebellion in company with his brother Edward, August 1864, and returned North at the close of the war, March, 1865. In 1868 went out to the Mississippi river. While there filled the berth of clerk, mate, carpenter, or engineer, as required. After three years on the Mississippi and the Ohio rivers, settled in Dunleith, Illinois and married on 8 January 1871, Eliza Penfield 2081, the third daughter of Edward Hall and Frances Penfield his wife, of Catskill, New York. In September, 1872, his new house was burnt down during the night. Mr Hingston, his wife and child had a narrow escape. In 1875 he returned to Buffalo, and was engaged as superintendent for dredging and submarine blasting and contracting. Joined the 74th Regiment N.G.S.N.Y. in 1878, commissioned 2nd Lieut. 5 June 1880 and 1st Lieut. 17 June 1852. Mr Hingston crossed the Atlantic three times since 1895, visiting England, ireland, Scotland, France, Belgium, Germany, Italy, Algiers and Gibraltar. His eight children;- Annie 594, Edward 595, George 596, Frank 597, William 598, Raymond 599 (dead), Howard 600 and Ralph 601. Residence 420 Ashland Avenue, Buffalo, New York, U.S.A.

541 Edward J Hingston

(HN#27) Second son of Edward 520 and Elizabeth 2060. Born 18 days after the death of his father at Thomastown, Maine, 22 January 1844 was taken to England by his mother in the following April. Educated at St Mary's in Liverpool and came to America arriving in Buffalo 8 May 1862. Learned the trade of shipbuilding with his uncles Hingston Bros and with his brother William went south during War returning to Buffalo at the close in 1865. In 1870 went into the employ of Clarke Douglas & Co Dredging Contractors, the last of the firm dieing in 1879. Mr Hingston bought the plant from the estate and has been very successful contractor for over 20 years and placed the firm Hingston and Woods in 1900 at the very head in its line owning 12 dredges, 5 large derricks, 3 submarine drills, 7 tugs, 34 mudscows also sandpumps, lighters etc with a capacity of over 19000 cubic yards per day. Has been engaged upon Canadian Government work on the Welland Canal for the United States Government at the Pariton River, St Lawrence River, Oswego, Charlotte, Oak Orchard, Olcote, Nelson, Niagara Falls, Tonawanda, Buffalo, Dunkirk, Erie, Astabula, Toledo, Detroit, Lake St Clair, St Marys River, The Soo, Lake Superior and Portage Lake Canal etc, aggregating many million dollars besides large contracts for the state of New York and the City of Buffalo. Mr Hingston married Miss Mary Reese 2082 of Buffalo the 22 July 1872 and has two daughters Louisa 602 and Genevieve 603. Residence 88 Oakland Place Buffalo NY.

542 Cuthbert Hingston

Eldest son of William 525 and Charlotte 2064 born in Buffalo NY 15 Nov 1851. Was a very bright and promising boy died of Diphtheria 30 Jan 1863 buried at Forest Lawn Cemetery. He was the last child of this branch of the family that died in 28 years (see #599)

543 Caroline Elizabeth Hingston

Eldest daughter of William 525 and Charlotte 2064 born at Buffalo NY 3 May 1853 married to Thomas B Reading of Aurora NY 4 Oct 1883. Is a great worker in and some time secretary of the Woman's Union. Now living in Buffalo NY..

544 Charlotte Hingston

Second daughter of William 525 and Charlotte 2064 born at Buffalo 5 Nov 1855. Died of Diphtheria 13 Jan 1863. Buried at Forest Lawn Cemetery.

545 William John Hingston

Second son of William 525 and Charlotte 2064 born at Buffalo 21 Aug 1857 has been engaged in the boat building trade in company with his father for a number of years, now living in Buffalo. Was married to Miss Minnie Child 2200 28 Nov 1894.

546 Lucretia Euphrosyne Hingston

Third daughter of William 525 and Charlotte 2064 born at Buffalo 6 Jun 1860. Married Millard Filmore Woodward of Buffalo 21 Oct 1893. Moved west where Mr Woodward was engaged in business in Los Angeles but Mr W dieing in 1895 she returned to Buffalo where she now resides.

547 Jane Hingston

Youngest child of William 525 and Charlotte 2064 born in Buffalo 16 Feb 1863. Married 6 Nov 1890 to Dr Jacob Harvey Burkhardt 2144 of Cleveland O. Now of Batavia NY where Mr Burkhardt has a large practice as a dentist. They have one son Richard Hingston 3205.

548 William Edward Hingston

(HN#28) Only son of John 526 and Charlotte 2065 born in Buffalo NY 29 Aug 1857. Was for some time in the Steamboat and RR ticket business in Buffalo. In 1880 he went to Europe and upon his return located in Chicago Ill in the Commission business. Mr Hingston married Miss Annie M Lee of Buffalo 2084 the 25 Oct 1876 she died at Sioux City Iowa 19 Jan 1890 and is buried in Forest Lawn Cemetery Buffalo. She left two children Lee 604 and Louisa 605. Mr Hingston is now in Boston Mas doing business as an expert accountant. He married secondly Miss Carrie Hall 2687 of Buffalo and has by her one son Townsend Hall 635.

549 Juliana Hingston

Eldest daughter of John 526 and Charlotte 2065 born in Buffalo 10 July 1859 Married Henry Hill 2085 and has issue Margery 3089, Helen 3090, Norman 3091 and Walter 3092. Mr Hill is now (1903) the US appraiser for the port of Buffalo NY.

550 Emma Bird Hingston

Youngest daughter of John 526 and Charlotte 2065 born at Buffalo 8 Sept 1864 graduated from the Normal School Buffalo June 1883 and was teacher in the public schools when she married Harrison Williams Blake 2086 1 Nov 1887. 700 Ambrose Hingston

701. Samuel James Hingston

Youngest son of James 206 and Catherine 2024; born at Cloyne, Ireland 1775. Married Miss Winifred Cavindish 2100 of County Cavin, Ireland, 1797 and had by her three children - Sarah 702, Thomas 703 and Samuel 704. Colonel, then Lieut. and Adjutant, Hingston came to Canada with the 100th (?) Regiment, which, at the close of the war became the 99th (?). He was in command of the infantry in Port Erie, Canada, when they bombarded the then village of Buffalo and Fort on the 17th March 1813. Some of the shells fell short, and, falling in the Niagara River, two of them, a six and a ten inch, were dug up by Hingston and Wood's dredge, then in charge of W.E. Hingston, the compiler of these notes, on the 22nd of June, 1889, 76 years afterwards, and are now in his possession.

Lieut. Hingston distinguished himself and earned his promotion at the Battle of Chippawa, near Niagara Falls, where he was wounded in the forehead and in the groin, 5 July, 1814. The British loss that day was 138 killed and 365 wounded. When the regiment was disbanded some time afterwards, he chose a very pretty place near Huntingdon, on the Chateauguay river. There he organized the Militia Force, Lord Dalhousie giving him command of the County of Huntingdon, and subsequently Sir James Kempt gave him Colonelcy of the whole County of Beauhamoes (Beauharnois?). He continued, to the end of his life, in 1830, to be lame from the wound he received at Chippawa.

The Colonel's wife having been dead some time, he married again, this time Miss Eleanor McGrath, 2101, of Montreal, in October, 1821, who died in 1866, having survived him 35 years. By the second wife he had two daughters - Eleanor 705 and Margaret 706, and two sons - William Hales 707 and Samuel James 708, the latter born six months after his father's death.

(A footnote on this page says Note: Edward H. Hingston, 3130, Winnipeg, now, 1903, in Oregon - it is not clear to what this refers)

Davidson, and all but one child survived the father, who died 23 January 1899.

707 William Hales Hingston

M.D., of Montreal, eldest son of Samuel 701 and Eleanor 2101. Born at Huntingdon, Canada, 29 June 1829. His father died some 18 months afterwards. He is widely known as W.H. Hingston, M.D., L.R.C.S.E., D.C., LL.D. etc., the most popular and able surgeon in Canada. He was invited by the American Consul General to go to Washington to assist in consultation with President Garfield's physicians, but he replied that he concurred in the opinion of Drs. Hamilton and Agnew; that there was already a sufficient number of able surgeons attending the wounded President, and that any further meddling might be disastrous.

Dr Hingston was twice mayor of Montreal, 1875 and 1876. "The Illustrated Paper of Montreal" of 7 April 1875, has a good likeness of him engrossed from a photo, also a brief sketch of his life, from which I quote the following:

"He was sent to a small grammar school in the neighbourhood, kept by a Rev. Mr. Williams, a Church of England clergyman, and afterwards by Mr. (now Sir) John Rose, and subsequently Mr. Anderson. During Mr. Rose's time he obtained the first prize in the Junior class, and during Mr. Anderson's incumbency, the prize among the seniors. Then at 13 he was sent to the Montreal College, where, at the end of the first year he obtained the prize in every branch, carrying three firsts and two seconds, while his chief opponent, the present Superior of the College, obtained the remaining two first and three second. The Rev. Mr. Villineure, one of the masters, often spoke of him as having been at that time full of fun and merriment, "un grand farceur" as he was then termed, and doing anything to create merriment or avoid a quarrel, but when a quarrel was forced upon him, never shrinking from the issue, no matter how uncertain it might appear. He afterwards spent a couple of years in studying pharmacy with R.W. Rexford, when he entered upon the study of medicine at McGill University. He graduated at the end of four years, and immediately left for England. He obtained the Surgeon's diploma of the University of Edinburgh. By the most rigid economy he succeeded in visiting England, Ireland and Scotland, and almost every country in Europe, spending the greater part of his time in hospitals, and bringing back with him diplomas from Scotland, France, Prussia, Austria and Bavaria, one, the membership of the Leopold Academy, purely honory and given only to authors, was the first ever obtained by a Canadian, Sir William Logan and T. Slerry Hunt being the next recipients of the honor. Much of his journeys in Europe were made on foot, an exercise in which he still excels, his travelling companion for the time being young Alexander (now Lord) Shand of Edinburgh and Mr P. Honeymeade of Glasgow. His utter abnigation of self and untiring zeal and benevolence during the cholera season of 1854 has built for him an extensive practice, besides a host of friends.

Soon after beginning practice, Mr. Hingston received unsolicited the appointment of Surgeon to the English speaking departments of the Hotel Dieu Hospital. Many of the more difficult and hazardous operations in surgery have been thus introduced by him to the profession in Canada, such as, for instance, excision of the knee joint, removal of the uterus and congenitalia, and acquired deformities, the successful removal of the tongue and lower jaw at the same time, etc. While visiting Europe in 1867 one of his masters, Professor (now Sir) James Simpson, paid a high tribute to Canadian surgeons in the person of Dr. Hingston, by inviting him to perform a surgical operation of difficulty on one of his (Sir James') patients; and on speaking of him a few weeks afterwards in a British medical journal of the time Sir James styles him 'that distinguished American surgeon lately amongst us'. He received the degree of D.C.L. from the University of Lennoxville (Bishop's University) in 1871, and in 1874 he was unanimously elected Governor of the College of Physicians and Surgeons of Lower Canada."

He was elected mayor of Montreal in 1875 by a very large majority. The same paper quoted above, speaking of it, adds: "The boldness and frankness of the new Mayor's inaugural address was of a character to call forth enconiums from the press generally, the Witness speaking of it as equalling Gladstone's efforts in clothing the dryest material in poetic language."

In surgery, Dr. Hingston has probably done as much work as any surgeon in America. When in Europe in 1866 (1886) this fact was recognized by the British Medical Association when electing him an honory member, the President of the Council, Sir Walter Foster, saying, "He is too well known to the members of this Association to require I should say anything regarding him. Dr. Hingston's reputation as a surgeon is not confined to Canada."

In July, 1892, Dr. Hingston went to England at the invitation of the British Medical Association to deliver the inaugural address on surgery before that great body. This is the first time Canada, or indeed America, has been so honored.

Dr. Hingston married, 16 September 1875, Margaret Josephine 2104, daughter of the Honorable D.A. McDonald, Lieut. Governor of Ontario. The ceremony took place in St. Michael's Church, Toronto, and the reception was held in the Government House.

On the Queen's birthday, 1895, Dr. Hingston was created a Knight Batchellor (Bachelor) at the same time as Lewis Morris, the poet, Dr. W. Russell, the war correspondent, Henry Irving, the actor, and Walter Besant, the author.

Sir William and Lady Hingston have issue - William 720, Donald 721, Aileen 722, Reginald 723, and Edmund 724, His residence is on the corner of Sherbrooke and Metcalf Sts., Montreal, Canada.

708 Samuel James Hingston

He married, 27 November 1856, Rebecca Cecilia Turney 2105, of Montreal. (see note) Mr. Hingston followed a drygoods or clothing business all his life, and has crossed the Atlantic 27 times to purchase stock, and was for many years senior partner in the firm of Hingston, Coy and Peake, Clothiers, Kansas City, Mo. Mrs Hingston died in 1899. Mr. Hingston and their son, James 725, survive her.

776 Thomas Hingston

Of Halwell, together with William of Blackhawton (sic - Blackawton?) 777, Philip of Woodleigh 778, and John of Morley 780, are the progenitors of the Hingstons in and around Brent, Stave (sic - does he mean Staverton?), Morley, Blackhawton, etc. During the time of this generation to which they belong, there was quite a break-up in the Hingston family, and whether these are brothers or cousins it is hard to say, for many of the same names settled in the south east. St any rate, these four are only one remove from the five sons of the house of Holbeton. Mr. Thomas Hingston married, 19 January 1706, Miss Elizabeth Perrin 2486, and had one child - Mary 779.

(Seattle, Wash., February 15 1907. Father told me, in 1883, that the five sons in his family were all older than the daughters and that he was the youngest of the sons; and my recollection is, he or his sister Eliza, with whom I corresponded for some time after his death, told me she was the youngest of the daughters. Lavinia Jane Hingston)

809 Edward Hingston

(HH#25) Son of Philip 806 and Dorothy 2108. Born at Mageressay in 1800. Went to London and learned the tailoring trade, there married Miss Mary Bowden 2156, of Manchester, about the year 1835, and while his first four children were quite young he moved to New York (city) U.S.A., and there opened a shop in Maiden Lane, between Nassau and William Sts. It was he who first got up the dress of the Hussars in the American Army, with assistance of General McCear, and Capt. Charles. In September, 1843, Messrs. Edward 520, William 525, John 526, and Samuel 527 called upon them, having just arrived on their way to Boston from Liverpool. It is a notable fact that the Williams and Edwards of the Hingstons are confined almost entirely to the two branches which I have designated the Brent branch and the Whitehall branch. Both descended direct from the Wonwell and Holbeton family, and separated about the year 1700 or 1730. These two branches lost sight of each other, and were not heard of until about 1870. Mr. William Hingston 823, who was travelling for a large nursery firm, on passing through Buffalo saw the sign and wrote to the firm of Hingston Brothers, boat builders. The correspondence was taken up a few years later by the writer and has been kept up ever since.

Mr Edward Hingston left the east and tried his fortune at Stockport, California, where, like many others, he dropped what he had and returned to New York, with a still larger family. The following are the names of his children, but not in the order of their birth - Edward 855, Richard 856, William 857, Mary 858, Sarah 859, Eliza 860, Charlotte 861, and Alice 862. Mr. Hingston died 29 June 1849.

810 Richard Hingston

(HH#24) Son of Philip 806 and Dorothy 2108. Born 1790. Learned boot and shoe making and moved to Bolonge (sic - Boulogne?), France, where he was in business for many years. He had one son, Richard, born in London. At present I know nothing further of him.

818 William Gillard Hingston

(HH#7) Youngest son of Philip 806 and Dorothy 2108. Born at East Alvington (Earlington), the home of his mother, 12 August 1807. Left home when quite young man and came to Canada, where he met and married, about 1835 (19 July 1836 has been added later) at Victoria Square, York County, Ontario, Miss Elizabeth Trudgeon 2112, who was born at or near Penzance, or Land's End, Cornwall, England, in 1819. Mr. Hingston afterwards took up a far (farm?) near Ainleyville, Dingle Post Office, Huron County, Ontario. The name of village and post office were afterwards changed to Brussels. Mr. Hingston had 17 children, 13 boys and 4 girls (a hand written note changes this to 14 boys and 3 girls) - Philip Henry 820, Richard Trudgeon 821, Rebecca 822, William 823, Mary 824, Thomas Lome 825, John 826, Edward Alfred 827, Arthur Augustus 828, George 829, Charles 830, Lavinia Jane 831, James 832, Edmund 833, Albert 834, Earnest 835, and one who died in infancy somewhere between William 823 and Arthur A. 828.

Mr. Hingston was well known and highly respected as one of the pioneer farmers of that section. He died 25 March 1884, surrounded by his wife and six of his then eight living children.

819 Thomas Richard Hingston

820 Phillip Henry Hingston

Mr. Hingston was for many years agent for farming implements at Brussels, but in 1889 moved to Abeline, Kansas, and engaged in the same business; afterwards moving to a fruit farm at Foothills, near Spokane, Washington.

832 James (West) Hingston

(HH#22) M.D., son of William Gillard 818 and Elizabeth 2112. Born at Victoria Square, 27 October 1857. Taught school near Brussels for two years; studied medicine at University of Michigan, Ann Arbor, Michigan, 1880-1881; graduated from Hahnemann Medical College, Chicago, 1882; practices at Peru, Illinois, until August of that year, then removed to North Platts, Nebraska; afterwards, in 1893, to Cheyenne, Wyoming; coming to Chicago in 1899; was Professor of Gynaecology in Dunham Medical College for two years; Professor of Materia Medica in Hahnemann College and Hospital 1903-1906; Professor of Surgery in Hering Medical College and Hospital, Chicago, 1906 ---. Married to Mrs. Jennie Weaver 2120, of North Platts, 1884 or 5. Their only child, Romaine James 853, was born at North Platts, 17 March 1886, and died at Goldfield, Nevada, 25 July 1905. Dr. Hingston married Miss Mae F. Engle (2845) of Chicago, 1 May 1884(?), and have children - Addison Wilbur Villele (866) , born 14 May, 1??5, and a son, born 17 May, 1906. 875 Peter Owen Hingston

(HH#11) Son of Peter Peter 803 and Grace 2258. Born at Plymouth 24 April 1826. Married about 1855 to Miss Harriet Ann Annis of Dodbrook 2261. The place still retains his name on the brass door plate. It was her and with him that John Hingston 890 lived the last years of his life, dying in 1883. Mr and Mrs Hingston had seven children, Peter 877, Sapho 878, Dorothy 879, Grace 880, Harriet 881, Reginald 914 and William 915. Mr Hingston died 25 March 1881, and is buried at Dodbrook cemetery. 972 William Henry Hingston

Only son of John 863 by his wife Caroline 2140. Born 3 April 1838. Was Major commanding the 58th Regiment in the first Boer war, and killed at the battle of Lang's Nec, 28 January 1881. Major Hingston entered the army as an Ensign in the 58th Foot in the year 1855. Before he was 22 years of age he obtained his company in the same regiment and thus became one of the youngest captains in the army. He served some years in India, but returned to England without having seen service in the field. In 1872 he obtained the brevet rank of Major in the army, and in 1875 was promoted to a Majority in his Regiment. After the disaster at Isandula, the 58th was one of the corps dispatched to reinforce Lord Chelmsford, and served with the forces under his command throughout the remainder of the Zulu War. Major Hingston was present at the battle of Ulunde, which virtually ended the campaign, and shortly afterwards the command of his Regiment devolved upon him and and he continued to exercise it until he fell whilst gallantly leading on his men in the action of January 28, 1881. He died of his wounds the next day. He is spoken of on page 346, chap 49 of "Recent British Battles" by James Grant. The Illustrated News of 26 February 1881 has a very good portrait of him from a photo by Mr. Jabez Hughes of the Isle of Wight. It is from an incident in this same fight and only a few moments before Major Hingston fell that the famous picture "Floreat Etona" was painted by Lady Butler, Poor Elwes fell among the Fifty-Eighty. He shouted to another Eton boy, Adjutant of the Fifty-Eighty, whose horse had been shot: "Come along, Monck! Floreat Etona! We must be in the rank!" And he was shot immediately. Lieuts Elwes and Monck belonged to the Grenadier Guards. The 58th lost 73 killed and 100 wounded during the engagement of about ten minutes. The Major was highly spoken of several times in General Orders. One item reads, "The bodies of Colonel Deane, Lieutenant Dolphin, Majors Pool and Hingston, and other officers were found by our burying parties well in front of the men they led." Major Hingston was buried in a grave just outside of the enemy's trenches, which marks the spot where he fell. I had the pleasure of meeting Mr. ... who had been a Serjeant under the Major, and who spoke very highly of him. He said he was very exclusive, very strict but just, and was beloved by his men.

973 Richard Hingston

Born in 1777. Died May 24th 1833 at Liscard.

Of Penryn. Born about 1700. The first account I have of him is from Cullerton's Armorial Report that his daughter Elizabeth 975 was married to Joseph Fox, Surgeon. He belongs to the same house as represented by 365 and 957.

993 George Bennett Hingston

Lieut. in the Royal Engineers, son of Richard 985 and Elizabeth 2599. Born 1 October 1867. Ensign 23 July 1887, Lieutenant 28 July 1890, and Captain 1 April 1898. Served in the Campaign on the N.W. Frontier of India in 1897-98 with the Tochi Field Force. Had his horse killed under him and was slightly wounded (Medal with Clasps). Now at the (illeg) Works, Allahabad, India (1903)

1351 Henry Hingston

Son of Thomas 1340 and Nancy 2647 born at Torquay 24 June 1828 and was married to Margaret Sewell in 1853. She died in 1855 without issue. He married secondly Miss Isabella Cooper (2648) the 22 July 1856 and by her had three children Thomas 1353, Mary 1354 and Joseph Cooper 1355. Mrs Hingston 2648 died 5 Oct 1863 and Mr Hingston married for the third time Miss Mary E Walter (2649) on the 12 Dec 1865 and by her had three children Laura 1356, Artemus 1357 and Hattie 1358. Spent his young days in one of the colleges of the southern States studying to be a priest in the R.C.Church but did not like it and returned to his father who was a government clerk at Kingstown Jamaica, thence to England about the time of his second marriage he came to America and his last three children were born there. The last I have heard of or from him he living with his daughter Mrs Grannis (1356) in Duluth.

1414 George Hingston

The eldest son of George 1405 and Mary 2276 was born at Dartmouth the 1 July 1825. He never married and was consular agent for France, Spain and the United States. The writer had the pleasure of meeting him in 1895 when he was 70 years old, hail and hearty and could not help noticing the "Hingston" likeness in him. He died the 25 June 1897 and is buried at St Stonestalls Church Yard where so many of the same family have found a last resting place. (The parish church of Dartmouth Townstall is St Clement - CJB)

1429 Edward Peron Hingston

(HO#21) Son of John William 1423 and Sarah 2216 was born in London 1824 and married in 1857 to a Miss Stago (2217) of Scotland. She and her four children survived him. Mr Hingston at one time while travelling as advance agent for Anderson the "Wizard of the North" passed a Samuel Hingston of Rochester (the writer's uncle??) and stopping turned around saw that Samuel H had done the same. Both walked up to each other and Mr E.P.H. held out his hand and said "Say stranger, what may your name be. Mine is Edward Hingston." and mine replied my uncle is sam Hingston they shook hands, had a pleasant afternoon together and found they were related through several generations apart yet the family likeness was so great that each noticed it. Mr E.P.H. never came near Rochester or Buffalo without letting the family fo Sam, John and William know of it. He also travelled with Artemus Ward (Brown) as his agent and they became very much attached to each other. He died 9 June 1876 leaving four children Charles 1435, Lillian 1436, Mrs Gebhart 1437 and John 1438.

1430 James Hingston

(HO#22) The second son of John 1423 and Sarah 2216 born in London. Moved to Melbourne Australia about 50 years ago. Travelled all over the globe for the Melbourne newspapers and wrote "Around the World in infrequented places". Latterly he settled down as a Notary Public and Attorney. Never married. Lived the last forty years at the one Hotel, the ... House. In 1900 he heard that his niece Lillian was in South Africa he sent for her to come to him, she did then they both visited England, he going back by himself. He again came to England in the spring of 1902 but was in poor health and died at Exmouth the 7 Mar 1902, leaving the bulk of his possessions to his niece Lillian. He was 72 years old at his death.

1480 William Hingston

Son of (? was married - crossed out CJB) the husband of Abigail 2095. I believe to be the same as William 8 the husband of Sibell 2858. The clerks in the churches at that time were not over careful in their spelling for I find the name Hingston spelt nineteen different ways. The register shows him to be the father of 1481 - 1484 and Will 8 and Sibell 2858 to be the parents of 1485 and 1486. The dates of birth for the six being 1649, 1650, 1654, 1656, 1658 and 1665. Both Williams were sojourners at Aveton Gifford and the deatn of only one William is recorded that is the 28 July 1669 and as William no 8 was born in 1640 he cannot have six children before the age of 29. (Note that this does not match what WEH has written for 8 William - CJB)

(HC#12) Son of Nathaniel 1586 and Elizabeth 2500 born 1 May 1813 and married 12 Oct 1838 to Mary Penning 2820 of Modbury and died 7 Sep 1896. For issue see 1639 and 1640.

1597 Nathaniel Hingston

(HC#4) Son of Hugh 1591 and his wife Mary 2466 of Aveton Gifford , born 9 March 1807 married Miss Jane Croker 2704 of Kingsbridge. Was in the Coast Guard service and stationed in the Silly (Scilly- CJB) Islands. He was described to me by Mr Hicks of St Hellars whose daughter married Mr George Hingston (829) of Canada as "A very tall and fine looking man with flaxen hair, and with a strong family likeness." He had four children, George Henry 1677, a daughter 1678, William 1598 and John 1599. His children were all born in Kingsbridge or the Silly Islands but emigrated to Ireland. He came with them or followed and died in Belfast.

1598 William Hingston

(HC#5) The son of Nathaniel 1597 and Jane 2704 was born 10 Sep 1840, married Miss Agnes Margaret Gillis (2703) of Liverpool in 1870 and is now the superintendent of the wood work and launching of the boats in Harland and Wolfe's yards Belfast where all the White Star liners are built he has seven children see 1679 to 1685.

1599 John Hingston

Brother of the above and son of Nathaniel 1597 and Jane 2704 was born 4 May 1843. He is in Belfast and with his brother William.

1639 Nathaniel Hingston

(HC#13) Of Malborough, son of Richard 1596 of Aveton Gifford and his wife Mary 2820 born 2 Jan 1839, baptised the 11 Feb 1839 and married 17 Dec 1865 to Miss Mary Grace Moore (2822) of the same parish. Mr Hingston is a mariner and Capt of his own boat and has been for many years. Is Alive and well and lives in his own house near the church in Malborough. He is known among his friends as "Cloudy" because of an expression he used often to make use of which was very appropriate while he was navagating the English Channel. He is still hail and well at the age of 67. His wife died in 1880. His only son (William Frederick) 1693 died 3 Feb last year and his yuoungest daughter Mary 1696 got married the year before so he is now practically alone. (Feb 1906)

1640 Richard Steer Hingston

Daughter of Abram 1731 and Sarah 2581 born 9 Feb 1812, married Robert Pound 2588 of the same parish the 9 April 1840 and died 29 Sep 1871. Their son William Hingston Pound 3214 is now (1906) in the grocery business at Thurlestone on the outside of his house which I believe was the home of his father there is a "Fuchia" growing that is over 60 years old the stem is about five inches through.

1766 William Hingston

Son of William 1752 was born in Sept 1841 and married the 18 Dec 1860 to Elizabeth Williams 2795 of Buckfastleigh. Mr Hingston is a mason and builder by trade. He moved to London where his eldest daughter (Elizabeth 1780) was born, thence to Plymouth in 1862, where the second child Evangelina 1781 was born and from there to Torquay where his eldest son William 1782 was born, then he emigrated to America and his two youngest children were born in Toronto Canada and at last settled down near Atlantic City, where he has a farm.

WEH used numbers in the range 2000 - 3000 for the spouses of Hingstons, and in a few cases he produced lists in a similar format to those for the Hingstons themselves. I have copied these where they exist but in most cases I have simply tried to list the names and their Hingston spouse, but not all the numbers are given In some cases it is possible to infer the number but there are several gaps in the list below which we may be able to fill when we go through his other papers.

2081 Eliza Penfield Hall

Of Orange, N.J., the daughter of Edward Hall, and Francis Penfield. Mr. Hall traces his lineage frem the Halls of Durham and Yorkshire, the same as Lyman Hall, one of the signers of the Declaration of Independence of the United states. Miss Hall was born 10 November, 1850. Married at Dubuque, Iowa, W. E. Hingston 540, the compiler of these records, the 8 January, 1871, and has a large family. See 594 to 601. Present address 420 Ashland Avenue, Buffalo,N.Y.

2082 Mary Reese

Of Trenton, N.J., born 9 June, 1849, and married 22 July, 1872, at Buffalo to E.J. Hingston 541. Has two daughters, see 602 and 603. Present address Niagara Hotel, Buffalo, N. Y.

2083 Thomas B. Reading

Of East Aurora, N.Y., now of Buffalo, born 17 June, 1850, and married 543 Caroline Elizabeth Hingston the 4 Ootober, 1883. Is foreman for the Lake Erie Dredging Co. His chief business is taking care of the diving and divers, while working on sunken boats and submarine work.

2084 Anna Matilda Lee

WEH did not appear to have used any higher numbers in the 2000 series. Numbers above 3000 were used for children of Hingston women, so they carried their father's surname. WEH did not produce listings about them so I have not attempted to extract a comprehensive list.
